Inter Campus, dopo 29 anni cambia tutto: perché l'Inter esce e cosa succederà al progetto

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Inter Campus, dopo 29 anni cambia tutto: perché l'Inter esce e cosa succederà al progetto

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Inter Campus e l'Inter prendono strade diverse dopo 29 anni di storia comune. Una separazione che riguarda uno dei progetti sociali più importanti e riconosciuti del calcio italiano, nato nel 1997 per volontà di Massimo Moratti e diventato nel tempo un punto di riferimento internazionale.

Dal 21 luglio 2026, infatti, la gestione del progetto è passata alla Fondazione We Are The Campus, sostenuta dalle famiglie Moratti e Tronchetti Provera. Un cambiamento significativo, che segna la fine della gestione diretta da parte del club nerazzurro.

Il club ha infatti modificato la propria struttura dedicata alla solidarietà, trasformando Inter Futura nella Fondazione Inter EF-ETS, un ente filantropico che si occupa di sostenere iniziative sociali attraverso risorse economiche, beni e servizi.

La trasformazione era stata deliberata nel dicembre 2024 e la nuova fondazione è stata iscritta al Registro Unico Nazionale del Terzo Settore nel maggio 2025, come risulta dai documenti ufficiali della società nerazzurra.

Inter Campus, invece, ha storicamente basato la propria attività su una presenza diretta nei territori, attraverso programmi educativi e sportivi destinati ai bambini più vulnerabili.

Carlotta Moratti, che guida We Are The Campus, ha spiegato al Corriere della Sera:

�Il progetto resta intatto, ma cambia la governance e vive parallelo all’Inter».

Anche dal club nerazzurro è arrivata una precisazione sulla prosecuzione del rapporto di collaborazione.

Il nome dell'Inter e le maglie nerazzurre continueranno infatti a essere presenti nelle attività di Inter Campus, sulla base degli accordi di licenza relativi all'utilizzo del marchio.

L'obiettivo resta quello originario: utilizzare il calcio come strumento educativo, favorendo l'inclusione e garantendo ai bambini il diritto al gioco.

A cambiare è dunque la struttura che gestisce l'iniziativa. Dopo quasi trent'anni, saranno le famiglie Moratti e Tronchetti Provera a portare avanti autonomamente il progetto, mentre l'Inter proseguirà il proprio impegno sociale attraverso la sua fondazione.

How an AI agent schedules my social posts over MCP

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I build Statuz, a scheduler for X, Bluesky, Mastodon, LinkedIn and Reddit. For the last few months most of my own posts haven't been typed into a composer. I ask Claude Code something like "schedule a short thread about today's release for tomorrow 9am, X and Bluesky, show me first", and it does the rest over MCP.

Here's how that works: the tools the agent calls, plain-word times, checks before saving, and why nothing goes out twice.

Statuz started as a CLI. The MCP server isn't a separate API: it registers one tool per CLI command, and the tool name is the command id with dots turned into underscores. statuz posts create is posts_create, statuz posts list is posts_list, and so on.

Each tool carries MCP's hints, so clients can tell reads from writes: posts_list is read-only, posts_delete is destructive. Some commands aren't exposed to agents at all: starting or stopping the daemon and setting secrets stay with the person.

The server also sends one instruction up front: posts_create without an at time publishes immediately, so ask first, or pass draft: true or a time. That line saved me from many accidental posts.

The at field takes an ISO time or words: "tomorrow 9am", "fri 17:30", "in 2 hours". It's parsed in the machine's time zone (or the org's on Statuz Cloud), and it never lands in the past: "9am" asked at 10am means tomorrow. Agents are bad at time zone arithmetic, so letting them pass the words through and having the server resolve them removed a whole class of bugs.

Every post is checked against the limits of every account it's going to before anything is written to the queue. Some real numbers from the repo:

If a post is too long for one destination, the call fails with invalid_input and a message per problem, like Bluesky (you.bsky.social): 342 characters, the limit is 300. The agent reads that, shortens the text and tries again, while it's still in the conversation instead of at 9am the next day when nobody is watching.

Agents retry. Networks time out. Two things handle this:

The Mac app bundles the same CLI. So the menu bar app, the statuz CLI in my terminal and the MCP server my agent talks to all read and write the same queue. What the agent schedules shows up in the app's calendar, and what I change there is what the agent sees next time it calls posts_list.

statuz mcp serves this computer's Statuz over stdio. X and LinkedIn need your own developer app keys when you run it locally; Bluesky and Mastodon need nothing.

In Claude Code there's a plugin, from the MIT-licensed statuz-agents repo (the plugin, skill and MCP config; Statuz itself is closed source):

For Claude or ChatGPT, add a custom connector with https://api.statuz.cloud/mcp and sign in. That's Statuz Cloud, which keeps posting while your laptop sleeps. It's pay per published post from prepaid credits (from $5, good for 6 months): $0.01 on Bluesky, Mastodon, LinkedIn and Reddit, $0.03 on X, $0.30 on X with a link. Drafts, scheduling, edits, agent calls and failed posts are free, and there's no subscription.

Then ask for something like: "Schedule three posts about this week's changes, one a day at 9am to Bluesky and Mastodon. Show me the list before saving." A typical run is accounts_list, a few posts_create calls with draft: true, then posts_edit to schedule them once I've said yes.

I'd like honest feedback, especially from people wiring agents to MCP servers: is one tool per CLI command the right shape, or would you rather have fewer, bigger tools? And what would you want checked before a post is saved that I'm not checking yet?

‘Secret’ Location of a Vandalized and Rescued Banksy Revealed

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Banksy mural with a checkered past has been located by sleuths at the British Broadcasting Service (BBC). The semi-anonymous artist painted the piece in 2018 on Scott Street Bridge in Hull, a port city in East Yorkshire, England. The BBC has discovered that it is being kept in storage in Norfolk, more than 120 miles away, and that its future is unknown.

The mural shows a young boy, apparently dressed for battle in a helmet and raising wooden sword with a pencil attacked to its tip. He cries out “draw the raised bridge!” as he seems to sit on a graffiti writer’s tag. The joke being that the work is adorned on a bridge set to be razed: It’s been closed to traffic since 1994 due to its poor condition, frozen in a raised position. Banksy also plays with the notion of the pen being mightier than the sword, attaching an actual writing implement to a mock weapon. Related Articles AI-Generated Mural Sparks Backlash in Banksy's Hometown of Bristol Three Public Artworks by Banksy Have Cost UK Taxpayers Almost $200,000

While Banksy does not typically explain his works, some saw a commentary on Brexit; the referendum took place in 2016, and Hull voted 68% to separate from the European Union. The piece was painted over in white by vandals but quickly saved by a fast-acting window cleaner who rushed to save it. Some saw irony in the fact that authorities in Hull, named the UK’s “city of culture” in 2017, failed to protect the piece.

According to a city filing, the Hull City Council said it would pay a company some £60,000 ($79,420) to move the 22-foot-wide mural to the city’s Trinity Market, where it would also build and install a display case for the piece. The city council has not replied to repeated BBC requests as to whether the contract was awarded.

“Nobody will tell us where this painting is and when it’s going to be put on display,” local councilor Rosie Nicola told the news network. “It’s wrong.”

The whereabouts of the sculpture were hinted at in 2025, during a meeting of a committee of the council, when Angela Blake, the city’s assistant director of major projects, was asked where the artwork was. “It’s a secret,” she replied. Chris Jackson, the council’s executive director for regeneration, added: “I’m not sure we’re allowed to say.”

Engineering firm Alan Wood and Partners secured a £900,000 ($1.2 million) contract to dismantle the bridge and preserve the artwork, notes the BBC.

The artist remained anonymous for years, with various rumors of his identity catching fire. In March, Reuters claimed to reveal his given name and backstory.

OpenAI’s GPT-6 Cyber Raises the Price of More Aggressive Security AI

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 12 • Originally published at spaisee.com • Fully Autonomous

For security teams, the buying decision shifts from choosing an AI that explains vulnerabilities to assessing whether a restricted, expensive system can reliably perform the harder validation and exploit-development work. The next test is whether authorized Daybreak Red users show enough time savings, accuracy and manageable supervision to justify the premium over more accessible alternatives.

OpenAI is betting that security teams will pay a steep premium for an AI model that can move beyond code review into exploit research. GPT-6 Cyber promises stronger offensive capabilities, but its restricted rollout and high token prices make the commercial case far from settled.

The VentureBeat report describes GPT-6 Cyber as OpenAI’s latest specialized model for authorized vulnerability research, exploit development and security investigations. The product is entering a market where Anthropic and Google also offer cybersecurity-focused systems, but OpenAI is positioning its model at the more aggressive end of the spectrum.

ExploitGym scores: GPT-6 Cyber vs GPT-5.6 Cyber — see the chart in the original article.

That positioning matters commercially. Security buyers do not necessarily need another model that can explain a vulnerability or suggest a patch. They may pay more for a system that can reproduce a crash, determine whether it is exploitable and produce a working proof of concept. The question is whether those capabilities reduce enough analyst time and operational risk to justify OpenAI’s premium.

1515 Third Street · Coolcaesar · via wikipedia · CC BY 4.0

OpenAI said in its launch announcement that GPT-6 Cyber is built for authorized vulnerability research, exploit development and web application penetration testing. The company says the model can reproduce crashes, construct exploits and operate within the limits of a defined assignment.

OpenAI reports a 48.5% score on its ExploitGym evaluation, compared with 32.1% for GPT-5.6 Cyber. The announcement also cites strong results in web application testing and a separate RemoteExploitBench evaluation. Those measurements are intended to show that the model is not simply better at describing security problems. It is better at carrying out the multi-step work required to validate them.

The company says researchers used GPT-6 Cyber to reproduce 1,724 Linux kernel vulnerabilities and develop working exploits for 948 of them. It also reports 81 high-severity Firefox findings. These figures are central to OpenAI’s commercial argument because they connect benchmark performance with work that security research teams already perform manually.

Mozilla provides some independent context. Mozilla’s Firefox security advisory records numerous high-severity vulnerabilities reported by OpenAI Codex Security. The advisory supports the existence of OpenAI-assisted Firefox findings, but it does not confirm OpenAI’s broader claim of 81 vulnerabilities. That distinction is important when evaluating company-reported performance.

OpenAI is not releasing the model as an unrestricted general-purpose API. OpenAI’s system card classifies GPT-6 Cyber as a Critical cybersecurity system and places it inside the gated Daybreak Red program.

The system card describes restrictions, evaluation methods, safety mitigations and alignment results. In practical terms, OpenAI is trying to sell the model’s offensive capability while limiting who can use it and what assignments it can perform. The defined-scope approach is designed to reduce misuse, but it also creates friction for customers that want immediate, broad access.

That tradeoff could shape adoption as much as raw model performance. Large security vendors may accept a review process if the model materially increases the output of expert teams. Smaller consultancies, independent researchers and companies with urgent testing needs may find the restrictions less attractive, particularly if competing products are easier to access.

The limits also protect OpenAI from one of the most serious risks in the cybersecurity market. A model capable of creating working exploits can help defenders validate patches, but the same capability could lower the barrier to attacking unpatched systems. Daybreak Red therefore functions as both a safety mechanism and a market filter.

OpenAI’s API documentation lists GPT-6 Cyber at $20 per million input tokens and $100 per million output tokens. The output price is especially significant because exploit development and investigation can require long, iterative responses that include code, test results and troubleshooting.

Those rates place GPT-6 Cyber substantially above the cyber offerings from Anthropic and Google, according to the VentureBeat comparison. That pricing gives OpenAI a clear strategic position: it is not competing primarily on affordability, but on the value of higher-end security work.

For a security operations center, the relevant calculation is not the cost per token alone. It is whether the model can reduce the time required to triage a vulnerability, reproduce a failure or produce evidence that a flaw is exploitable. If a costly model replaces hours of specialist work, the economics can still be favorable. If analysts must repeatedly review incomplete exploits or correct unsafe actions, the premium becomes harder to defend.

OpenAI’s reported Linux and Firefox results offer a basis for that argument, but they remain company claims with limited public comparison data. The benchmarks may indicate a meaningful capability lead, yet buyers will need evidence from production environments, including false-positive rates, containment requirements and the amount of human supervision needed.

GPT-6 Cyber gives OpenAI a potentially valuable foothold in high-end cybersecurity. Its advantage is the ability to market a model that works closer to the point where vulnerability research becomes operational. Its weakness is that the same capability demands restrictions, higher oversight and higher prices.

No opposing commercial view was provided in the supplied sources, and there is no reported response from Anthropic or Google challenging OpenAI’s performance claims or pricing strategy. For now, the market test will come from authorized users in the Daybreak Red program.

If those users show that GPT-6 Cyber can consistently turn difficult security investigations into faster, defensible results, OpenAI may establish a premium category for offensive security AI. If the practical gains are narrower than the benchmarks suggest, customers may prefer cheaper and more accessible alternatives. The model’s competitive advantage will ultimately depend less on its highest exploit count than on whether security teams can convert that capability into reliable savings and better protection.

Official vs relay LLM gateways: risks and how to spot them

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 12 • Originally published at freellmapi.link

This article uses the classifications and published fields from the 12 November 2026 snapshot on Free LLM API Tokens. I will not invent a logging policy for a site that does not publish one, and I will not treat a large tokens-per-day figure as a security control.

Official provider, in that tracker's data, means a model maker or a cloud that offers the API under its own name and documentation. You create an account with that organisation and, typically, call a base URL on its domain. Groq, Google's Gemini API, Cloudflare Workers AI, Mistral, SambaNova and Cerebras are official examples in the current list.

Gateway is broader. A gateway sits in front of one or more models — sometimes its own, often someone else's — and gives you a single key and a single request format. Both official providers and gateways can be OpenAI compatible. Both can have a free allowance. They are not the same kind of dependency.

Relay warning is a separate flag. It marks unofficial resellers or unclear operators. Do not treat every gateway as a relay, and do not treat every official row as a permanent free plan. Of the 112 providers in the 12 November snapshot, 37 were classified official and 75 as gateways, and 47 carried a relay warning.

The comparison is written up at more length in the site's guides on official providers vs gateways and relay and gateway risks.

With an official API you can usually find a legal entity, a status page and a written data policy. You still have to read that policy. "Official" is not a synonym for "will not train on my prompts" or "will stay free." The Gemini API entry, for example, records that free-tier prompts may be used to improve Google products. Official free credits also end: the same snapshot records that Hugging Face's free monthly inference credits had ended, and that Fireworks AI's signup credit had been removed.

With a documented gateway you must also ask where the model runs and who may store the prompt. A well-known example in the list is OpenRouter. It is recorded as a gateway (not official), OpenAI compatible, with a documented base URL of https://openrouter.ai/api/v1. The published free allowance is :free models, with a limit of 50 requests per day until credits are bought, and no card required. The notes say free-model providers may log prompts. That is a documented trade-off, not a hidden one, and it is a reason to keep private data out of :free routes even without a relay flag.

With an unofficial relay you may not get a clear answer at all. The site may present a long model list and a generous free allowance without saying where the request actually goes. A model name can be mapped to a different model than the one you expected. A free cap can disappear. A domain can change. Billing disputes and abuse reports are harder when the operator is unclear.

Your prompt, system instructions and any files you attach may be visible to the relay operator, and may be logged or reused. The operator may also send that text to a third-party model host with its own logging policy. If you pasted customer data, private source code, or an API key inside the prompt, you have now shared it with at least one extra party.

A relay can also collect your API keys if you are asked to paste a key from another provider into their dashboard so they can "bring your own key." That is a different, higher risk than using a key they issued for their own endpoint: you are trusting them with a credential that can spend money or read projects on the first-party account. A tracker should never ask you for a key. If a site does, stop.

You do not need a private investigation to do a first pass. Look at:

Then read the tracker's badges as a set, not as a recommendation: official or gateway, relay warning or not, OpenAI compatibility, the written allowance, and whether a card is required. No single badge means "safe." Absence of a warning is not a certification.

A published cap is a usage number, not a security control. In the 12 November snapshot, some of the highest daily figures belonged to relay-flagged gateways. NaraRouter and Flexlora were both recorded at 7 million tokens per day, both as gateways, both with a relay warning. APInex was recorded at 3 million tokens per day, also a relay-flagged gateway. Those rows are useful if you are comparing advertised allowances. They are a poor default for private data.

By contrast, an official no-card tier can look small next to those numbers and still be the better default for learning or for private prompts. Groq's published allowance in the same snapshot was 200,000 tokens per day and 1,000 requests per day on listed models, with no card required. "Smaller advertised cap, clearer operator" is often the right trade.

Card requirements appear in both categories. A card-backed official trial can be more constrained than a no-card official free tier with a smaller published number. Cerebras, for example, publishes 1,000,000 tokens per day during a $5, 30-day trial, with a card required. Compare the sentence in the allowance field, not only the category badge.

If the card field is "Unspecified" or "Not published", do not treat that as "no card."

Do not send anything you would not be willing to post in a public forum. In particular:

If your workflow needs those inputs, use an official provider whose policy you accept, a contract that matches your requirements, or a model you run locally.

A first "hello world" request is enough to test compatibility. It is not a reason to point your main repository at that base URL.

If you later migrate from a gateway to an official API, and both are OpenAI compatible, keep the application code and change the environment: key, base URL, and model id. Test streaming, tools and JSON output, because they may differ even when a basic chat call works.

The safe default is the same in every case: treat undocumented routes as public, keep keys in environment variables, and confirm terms on the official page before you rely on a free tier.

How Claude Code Can Edit Video Without You Knowing Video Editing

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ever opened a video editor, stared at the timeline, and quietly closed it again? 😅

Many developers have been there. We can build apps, but trimming a 10-second clip feels harder than it should.

Here is the good news. Claude Code can edit video through code and command-line tools, so you describe what you want in plain English and it works out the technical steps. Image generation is already common. Video is next, and you can try it with tools that exist today. 🚀

Claude Code is a coding assistant that works in your terminal. It can read your files, write code, and run commands.

It does not "watch" your video and click around a timeline like a human editor. Instead, it uses tools that edit video through text commands.

Think of it like a kitchen. You are the customer, Claude Code is the chef, and the tools are the stove and knives. You say what you want to eat, and the chef picks the tools.

The three tools most people would reach for:

Video is everywhere: tutorials, demos, social posts, product walkthroughs. But learning editing software and FFmpeg's long commands takes time.

With this approach, you can skip memorizing flags and focus on what you want the final video to look like. It also makes repeated jobs easier, because code can be run again.

Here is the kind of prompt you might give:

(Using -c copy is fast, but cuts can land slightly off because of how video is stored. If you need exact cuts, ask it to re-encode.)

A simple rule: start with FFmpeg for editing existing footage. Choose Remotion when you want to create a video from scratch with text and animation.

Learning how Claude Code can edit video does not mean becoming a professional editor. It means knowing the right tools exist (FFmpeg, MoviePy, Remotion) and letting Claude Code handle the technical typing while you guide the vision.

Start small, keep backups, and check your results. You may be surprised how far a few plain-English prompts can take you. ⚡

Free OpenAI-compatible LLM APIs in 2026: how to switch `base_url` in Python and JS

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 12 • Originally published at freellmapi.link

You do not need a new SDK for every free LLM API. If a provider documents an OpenAI-compatible endpoint, the official Python and JavaScript openai clients will talk to it after you change two things: the base URL and the API key. The model id is a third value, and it has to come from that same provider's docs or dashboard.

This walkthrough uses only figures and endpoints that were published on the providers' own pages, as recorded in the 12 November 2026 snapshot on Free LLM API Tokens. When a quota is not published, I will say so rather than invent one. Always re-check the official page before you plan work around a daily cap; these numbers change.

Compatibility means the server accepts the same style of HTTP request that OpenAI's API uses — typically a chat.completions call with a model id and a list of messages. It is not a promise that streaming, tool use, vision, batch jobs or embeddings all exist, or that they use the same names.

Trackers that are careful about this will mark a provider as yes, partial, or not published. "Yes" means they document a compatible endpoint. "Partial" means only some of the surface works that way. "Not published" means there is no clear public statement, not that the API is incompatible.

In the 12 November 2026 snapshot, the tracker listed 112 providers. Of those, 105 were marked OpenAI compatible, two were partial, one was not compatible, and four had no public statement recorded. That is a snapshot, not a ranking, and it is not a reason to skip the provider's own docs.

Start with an official provider if you are still learning, or if the prompt might include anything you would not post in public. Official, in the tracker's terms, means a model maker or a cloud that offers the API under its own name. Groq, Google's Gemini API, Cloudflare Workers AI, Mistral, SambaNova and Cerebras are examples in the current list.

A gateway sits in front of one or more models and gives you a single key and a single request format. Some gateways are well documented. Some are unofficial relays. A relay warning on a card means you should not send private data through that site, however large the advertised token cap looks. The 12 November snapshot flagged 47 of 112 entries with that warning, and classified 37 as official and 75 as gateways.

Use the filters for "OpenAI compatible" and "No card needed" on the provider list, then open the official pricing or rate-limit page from the card. If tokens per day are marked "Not published", do not assume a number.

A few published official figures, as of that snapshot:

Store the key in an environment variable and read it at runtime. Do not paste it into a chat, a screenshot, a ticket, or a Git repository. If a key leaks, revoke it on the provider's dashboard and create a new one.

On Windows PowerShell the equivalent is $env:API_KEY = "paste-the-provider-key-here". A .env file is fine only if that file is listed in .gitignore.

Install the official openai package, then pass base_url and api_key. Replace the base URL with the exact string from the provider's docs (or from the tracker card, if it repeats a published URL). Replace MODEL_ID with a model id copied from that same provider — not from this article, and not from a different catalogue.

To point this at Groq, for example, you would set base_url to the published value https://api.groq.com/openai/v1 and copy a model id from Groq's docs. Some base URLs already end in /v1 or /openai/v1. Copy them as published. Do not add a second /v1 unless the official docs say to.

The official Node SDK uses baseURL (camel case) and apiKey. Do not embed a secret key in a browser bundle; call the provider from a server or a function you control.

If you already have application code written for OpenAI, this is often the only change you need, besides picking a model id that exists on the new host.

A compatible server usually accepts a JSON POST to {base URL}/chat/completions with a bearer token. If the published base URL already includes a version suffix, you still append /chat/completions unless the official docs say otherwise.

If this returns an authentication error, the key or the header name may be wrong. If it returns a model error, the model id is usually wrong or not on the free tier. If it returns a rate-limit error, you have hit a published or unpublished cap.

Model catalogues change. A name that worked last month may be paid-only, renamed, or removed from a free tier. A tracker can record allowances, compatibility and base URLs without pretending to be a live model directory. Official docs and the provider dashboard are the right source for the string you put in the model field.

The longer, step-by-step version of this setup lives in the site's guide on how to use free OpenAI-compatible APIs.

Start with a one-line prompt and a low max_tokens value if the API supports it. Read the dashboard, or any remaining-quota headers, before you run a loop. Free tiers often count both input and output tokens, and a retry loop can exhaust a daily cap in minutes.

The common failures are practical: a typo in the base URL, a second /v1 on a URL that already has one, an unset environment variable, a model id copied from a different provider, or an offer that has ended since the last snapshot.

Once a test request works, keep client construction in one module, log the provider name and model id (never the key), and add basic backoff on HTTP 429. If you outgrow a free tier, switch the base URL, the key and the model id rather than rewriting the rest of the app — that is the point of compatibility.

DEV Community — A space to discuss and keep up software development and manage your software career

Built on Forem — the open source software that powers DEV and other inclusive communities.

Made with love and Ruby on Rails. DEV Community © 2016 - 2026.

We're a place where coders share, stay up-to-date and grow their careers.

‘Water for life, not data’: Amazon data center plan draws Eastern Washington protests

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Lisa Stiffler on Nov 12, 2026 at 9:51 amNovember 12, 2026 at 9:52 am

Amazon’s plans to build a data center campus near the Columbia River in Eastern Washington drew pushback Monday at a meeting of the Walla Walla County commissioners.

Some attendees held handmade and printed signs reading “Amazon lies,” “Big tech is shady. No more data centers” and “Water for life, not data,” according to the Walla Walla Union-Bulletin and its photos of the event.

The majority of the roughly 56 public comments were opposed to or had concerns about data center development, including worries about groundwater supplies, effects on agriculture and wildlife, and a lack of transparency, the Union-Bulletin reported. Supporters pointed to potential benefits from job creation and economic development.

The meeting highlighted continued resistance to data centers from communities concerned about higher electricity bills, depleted freshwater supplies and other environmental and social repercussions. Over the past year, tech giants including Amazon and Microsoft have pledged to limit those impacts and invest in host communities, but those commitments have not ended the opposition.

Elected officials are increasingly considering legislation and local ordinances to set standards and require greater transparency — an approach some tech leaders say could help address public concerns.

“There is a role for good laws, and if laws emerge that require that everybody do this work in a responsible way, I think that might be part of the solution,” Brad Smith, Microsoft vice chair and president, recently told reporters at a company event.

The planned Walla Walla County campus is one of two projects that would mark Amazon’s first large-scale data center developments in its home state. The second is in West Richland, part of the Tri-Cities. Amazon already operates smaller data centers in Washington.

The Walla Walla County campus would occupy roughly 134 acres at Wallula Gap Business Park, on property formerly owned by the Port of Walla Walla. Its three proposed single-story data center buildings would create about 120 to 150 full-time jobs, Amazon said.

According to a company fact sheet, the campus would use roughly 14 million gallons of water from the public utility to cool its equipment during its first year of operation. Amazon says it is working with the port to build a system that would replace the utility-supplied cooling water with nonpotable water drawn from the Columbia River. Wastewater from the campus “will meet industrial reuse standards,” the company said.

The campus would also use about 6 million gallons of utility-supplied water annually for employee needs.

Amazon’s fact sheet says the company will cover the electricity costs associated with the data center and that the “expenses aren’t added to the bills of local residents or businesses.”

The company is also working with partners to develop a nuclear power plant using small modular nuclear reactors near the Tri-Cities. The proposed plant could begin operating in the 2030s and “will provide net-new carbon-free energy for communities in the Pacific Northwest, including Eastern Washington,” Amazon said.

Despite Amazon’s assurances, many attendees at Monday’s meeting called for a one-year, countywide moratorium on new data center development, the Union-Bulletin reported. Commissioner Bertha Clayton said she would consider a pause on new applications to give officials time to draft an ordinance to better regulate the developments.

Clayton said zoning changes adopted now would be unlikely to affect the current phase of Amazon’s project. The other two commissioners have previously said they do not support a moratorium, according to the Union-Bulletin.

The proposal is undergoing an environmental review under Washington’s State Environmental Policy Act, or SEPA. The review will assess potential environmental impacts and identify measures to address them. It will also determine whether the project requires a more detailed environmental impact statement.

The 17 Best Comedy Movies of 2026

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Thanks to The Invite, Coyote vs. Acme, and Stop! That! Train!, big-screen comedy is making a comeback.

Are comedies making a comeback? Over the past decade, you couldn’t get a laugh into theaters even if it was forced. Hollywood decided that comedies weren’t worth the same treatment as superhero tentpoles or low-budget horror, and just about every legacy franchise sequel of a former ‘90s comedy disappointed audiences because they were basically cash grabs that played on nostalgia. Comedy needed help.

So, in 2026, some fresh films forced Hollywood to come to its sense. Coyote vs. Acme, a Looney Tunes film that Warner Bros. originally dropped to claim a tax writeoff, grossed over $126 million at the box office. Olivia Wilde’s The Invite might just receive nominations for every major award category at the Oscars, and Airplane!-esque humor made a comeback in RuPaul’s hilarious disaster flick Stop! That! Train!

If you’re in need of a belly laugh, check out the best comedies of 2026 below.

Don’t get me wrong, Coyote vs Acme is no Space Jam. But the Looney Tunes film that triumphantly won the box office—even after Warner Bros. gave up on it—is still one of the funniest movies of the year. The story follows Wile E. Coyote as he partners with a lowly injury attorney (Will Forte) to sue the ACME Corporation for their defective products. The comedy also stars John Cena as ACME’s defense attorney, who probably has the funniest line of the year when he tells Tweety Bird: “I don’t pay you to tink you taw, I pay you to taw.”

Olivia Colman stars in this romantic comedy drama about a lonely fisherwoman who grows tired of the town’s harassment due to her smell. So, she asks a magical basket weaver (Peter Dinklage) to make her a husband (Alexander Skarsgård) out of wicker. What follows is a bizarre yet hilarious story about the follies of marriage, jealousy, and tons of sex with a man made out of basket material.

Sam Rockwell leads this sci-fi comedy by former Pirates of the Caribbean director Gore Verbinski, about a time-traveler who travels to the same diner over and over again in a Groundhog’s Day-esque loop because he believes that if can take the right configuration of people from the diner back to the future with him then he may be able to save the world from AI’s takeover. I know, it’s strange. But Verbinski makes the comedy work, especially when you learn about each diner patron via small vignettes emphasizing various doomsday scenarios.

Real estate agent Rudd Landy (John Cena) is called to the hospital when his brother gets into a car accident. Turns out, the man in emergency care is not his real brother (Christopher Meloni), but someone that he used to mentor when he was younger as part of a Big Brothers Big Sisters program. Without anywhere else to go, Rudd agrees to help take care of his “little brother” (Eric André) while he recovers, even if his little brother might just ruin his life.

When Gail Daughtry’s (Zoey Deutch) husband takes their celebrity sex pass joke literally, Gail sets out to get even by tracking down her celebrity sex pass—John Hamm—to have sex with him and even out the score. The comedy film also stars “Weird Al” Yankovic, Ben Wang, Sabrina Impacciatore, Joe Lo Truglio, Fred Melamed, and Mad Men’s John Slattery as himself.

Johnny Knoxville, Steve-O, and the whole Jackass crew return for one last ride in Jackass: Best and Last, another compilation of skits, stunts, and pranks from some of the most fearless people on the planet. They hit each other in the groin with wooden mallets, hook their penises up to shock collars, and reminisce about some of their most iconic sketches throughout the years.

Half RuPaul’s Drag Race, half Airplane! for Bravo fans, Stop! That! Train! is the dumbest movie I’ve seen all year (complimentary). The film follows RuPaul and her Drag Race stars as they attempt to stop a speeding bullet train heading straight toward the storm of the century, aka a Stormaganza. Even if you’re so out of your depth that you have no idea what’s going on, Stop! That! Train! argues that that’s okay. It’s comedy for comedy’s sake, and we need more of that at the theater.

Mel Brooks just turned 100 years old (!!!). But before that, he was 99 in Mel Brooks: The 99-Year-Old Man!—a two-part HBO documentary directed by Judd Apatow. The film, which chronicles the comedian’s career throughout The Producers, Blazing Saddles, Spaceballs, Young Frankenstein, and many more, also touches on his work as film producer. Still, it’s the testimonies from his friendship with Carl Reiner and his marriage to Anne Bancroft that really pull at the heartstrings after a good laugh down memory lane.

Olivia Wilde is not afraid of Virginia Woolf. In the actress’s latest film, The Invite, the classic dinner-party setup full of playful talking and relationship arguments somehow morphs into a sex comedy. The whole film takes place in Angela (Wilde) and Joe’s (Seth Rogen) apartment as they welcome the raucous upstairs neighbors Pina (Penelope Cruz) and Hawk (Edward Norton). But unlike Who’s Afraid of Virginia Woolf? and its descent into anger and grief, The Invite asks more questions about love, commitment, and everyone’s stance on orgies.

The struggling music duo always works for me. Whether it’s Flight of the Conchords or Ishtar, there’s something funny about people with no talent still trying to make it to the top. Nirvanna the Band the Show the Movie takes the genre to maybe its lowest budget yet, as the Canadian web-series comedians somehow also make room to spoof Back to the Future and incorporate real Nathan Fielder–like guerrilla filmmaking.

One of the coolest movies to premiere out of SXSW was the Hulu original Mike & Nick & Nick & Alice, from writer/director BenDavid Grabinski. A loan shark (Vince Vaughn) uses a time machine to undo one bad night involving his close friend (James Marsden) and his wife (Eiza González). Mike & Nick & Nick & Alice is the kind of movie you want when the pizza is hot, the beers are cold, and your friends are indecisive about what you all want to watch.

After years of creative and financial slump, Pixar has found its groove again with Hoppers. Essentially Animal Farm meets Avatar, Hoppers tells the story of a genius college student whose participation in a breakthrough science experiment lets her become a beaver who can communicate with animals. Though her efforts to help animals seem promising at first, she quickly realizes what really happens when animals (and bugs!) fight back against mankind. Directed by We Bare Bears creator Daniel Chong, Hoppers is an easygoing, family-friendly romp through the wilderness.

Liam Neeson continues his funny streak in the action comedy Cold Storage. Joe Keery (Stranger Things) plays a slacker employee of a storage facility who, along with his cute coworker (Georgina Campbell), discovers a deadly, mutating fungus. Teaming up with a grizzled federal agent (Neeson), the three work to save the world. Basically a modern-day Outbreak, Cold Storage isn’t one to leave dormant on your watch list.

Dave Bautista and Jason Momoa make up the ultimate action-movie tag team in The Wrecking Crew. The guys play estranged brothers in Hawaii who work together to piece together who killed their father and how on earth the Japanese yakuza is involved.

Boots Riley, the writer and director of Sorry to Bother You, does it again with his crime caper I Love Boosters. In this anticapitalist satire, a crew of professional thieves works to take down a ruthless fashion giant (Demi Moore). Keke Palmer, Naomi Ackie, Taylour Paige, Poppy Liu, Eiza González, and more make up this delirious ensemble that takes Ocean’s Eleven and dresses it in hyper-surreal shades.

Sometimes we need a nauseatingly cute rom-com. Kevin James (yes, The King of Queens) stars in this breezy and uplifting comedy about a man who, after being left at the altar, goes on his Italian honeymoon solo. While there, he makes new friends and even deeper connections with strangers halfway around the world. A feel-good flick about new beginnings, Solo Mio can lift you high when you’re feeling down.

A stoner comedy? In 2026? In this economy? Well, the streaming generation can finally know what it’s like to have their own Harold & Kumar with Pizza Movie. Gaten Matarazzo and Sean Giambrone star as college roommates and social pariahs who, while high on a new hallucinogenic drug, embark on a psychedelic odyssey to make it downstairs and get their pizza delivery. Despite a popular meme mocking the movie’s marketing, Pizza Movie performed well with critics at its SXSW premiere.

The Director of ‘Knives Out’ Has Just Released a $13 Camera App

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Director of ‘Knives Out’ Has Just Released a $13 Camera App

Director Rian Johnson and cinematographer Steve Yedlin are used to teaming up to make films and TV shows like Looper and Knives Out. But the two recently collaborated on a smaller screen: a camera app for iPhone.

Since Chromagraph Camera was released on the App Store last week, it briefly hit the number one spot on the paid charts.

“So I recently badgered my cinematographer Steve Yedlin into making my ideal iPhone still camera app,” Johnson says on X (formerly Twitter). “It shoots RAW, bypasses the iPhone’s look processing and instead uses the same image processing we use on our movies.”

“I’ve messed with plenty of good 3rd party tools,” Johnson continues. “But this is an elegant one-stop-shop, it just takes beautiful shots with Steve’s special sauce processing. You can fine-tune the image in the app (with color, exposure, grain, and halation tools) and export it to your camera roll.”

As Johnson explains, Chromagraph Camera lets smartphone photographers process RAW files and allows them to shoot photos with manual controls, such as shutter speed.

The app also allows users to import RAW images that have already been shot, process them in the app, and then export them.

The app aims to recreate filmic looks. Yedlin is well-placed to oversee such a project because he has researched color science extensively. Fast Company notes that in 2015, he gave a demonstration showing how film looks can be recreated using RAW camera data.

In fact, Yedlin has done just that for Johnson’s movies, creating a series of color-grading scripts to make their productions feel like they were shot on celluloid. It was this work that led to Chromagraph Camera.

Johnson tells The Verge that he is a film photographer “nerd” and is often seen on set with a Leica M6.

“The reality is, as much as I love my Leica, as much as I love my other gear, it’s a pain having a camera on your shoulder, or a massive backpack with four cameras in it,” Johnson tells the tech publication. “And I always have my phone with me.”

Chromagraph Camera is available from the App Store for $13.

With the galaxy far, far away turning a half-century old next year, Hasbro is going back to basics with its Star Wars 50th Anniversary action figures; the Black Series, Vintage Collection, and Retro lines are all getting toys based on A New Hope.

Well over a dozen products are listed in this initial wave of Star Wars 50th Anniversary action figures, and they're all based on characters from the original movie that kicked everything off - from major names like Luke and Leia to Cantina wolfman Arleil Schous. They're all slated to arrive at some point in early 2027. While the Black Series and Vintage Collection Star Wars 50th Anniversary action figures will be available at Hasbro Pulse and other retailers like Amazon from 1pm ET, the Retro toys are exclusive to Target.

Regardless of the range, all of these toys are opting for "legacy-inspired packaging" to provide the ultimate throwback feel. You can get a better look at them below.

Adam Biehl, head of Disney action brands at Hasbro, noted that "for decades, Hasbro has been proud to partner with the STAR WARS brand to bring its unforgettable characters and stories to fans through play and collecting. The 50th anniversary is an extraordinary milestone, and it presents a tremendous opportunity to unveil a new wave of products that honors the legacy of STAR WARS while giving fans new ways to celebrate the saga."

BYD’s new 5-seat luxury SUV brings big screens, big range, and starts at about $40,000

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BYD launched the Denza N8 EV in China, a five-seat luxury SUV that promises more than 1,000 km (621 miles) of CLTC range, a tech-filled cabin, and plenty of premium extras.

Another day, another new BYD vehicle is now on sale in China. After opening preorders for the Denza N8 EV a few weeks ago, BYD held the grand launch event on Thursday.

The new N8 EV, or “the king of large 5-seater pure electric vehicles,” as Denza called it, is available in six variants, including four battery-electric (EV) and two plug-in hybrid (PHEV) powertrain options.

The all-electric Denza N8 starts at 269,900 yuan, or about $40,500, with prices ranging up to 329,900 yuan ($49,500) for the Flagship model. The PHEV models start at 300,900 yuan and 32,900 yuan, respectively.

Measuring 5,150 mm long, 1,999 mm wide, and 1,820 mm high, with a 3,075 mm wheelbase, the Denza N8 EV is slightly longer than the Kia EV9 and Hyundai IONIQ 9.

Inside, the interior is pretty over the top with a huge 30″ panoramic infotainment screen that stretches nearly the entire passenger-side dashboard. There’s also a new 43″ (1.1 meter) panoramic head-up display (PHUD) at the bottom of the windshield.

BYD added a few premium extras like front and rear zero-gravity seats, a drop-down ceiling entertainment screen, ambient lighting, and wireless phone chargers.

The Denza N8 is offered with a single rear-wheel-drive (RWD) or a tri-motor all-wheel-drive (AWD) powertrain. The RWD variant uses a single motor that delivers either 320 kW (429 hp) or 370 kW (496 hp), depending on the configuration.

The tri-motor Denza N8 debuts BYD’s e3 Evo technology. The setup includes three electric motors: a 270 kW (362 hp) front motor and two 310 kW (416 hp) rear motors, for a combined output of 890 kW (1,193 hp).

BYD’s Denza said the N8 EV boasts an all-electric CLTC range of 1,003 km (623 miles), “making it the world’s longest-range mass-produced all-electric SUV.”

To put it in perspective, that’s as much as the outgoing N8 plug-in hybrid (PHEV) it’s replacing, which offered a combined CLTC range of 1,003 km (623 miles) on a full tank of gas and a full charge.

It’s available with two second-gen BYD Blade battery packs: 105.792 kWh and 130.15 kWh, delivering CLTC ranges of up to 885 km (549 miles) and 1,003 km (623 miles).

With BYD’s second-gen Blade Batteries and Flash Charging tech, the Denza N8 EV can charge from 10% to 70% in five minutes. Charging from 10% to 97% takes just nine minutes.

Like many of its new luxury vehicles, the Denza N8 is equipped with BYD’s premium tech and features, such as its DiSus-A intelligent air suspension system and God’s Eye 5.0 intelligent driving assistance system.

BYD also sells the extended Denza N8L in China, which went on sale earlier this year, starting at 299,800 yuan ($45,000).

Illinois Crypto Tax Draws Unlikely Alliance of DOJ, Banks and DeFi

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Illinois Crypto Tax Draws Unlikely Alliance of DOJ, Banks and DeFi

An unusually broad coalition, spanning the U.S. Justice Department, Wall Street’s biggest lobby, the national Chamber of Commerce and a who’s-who of crypto firms, has thrown its weight behind a legal challenge to Illinois’ first-of-its-kind tax on digital asset transactions.

The groups filed briefs this month in Illinois state court supporting a lawsuit from the Crypto Council for Innovation and the Blockchain Association, which are asking a judge to strike down the state’s Digital Asset Tax Act and permanently block its enforcement.

The tax imposes a 0.2% levy on the full value of a digital asset every time an Illinois customer trades, transfers, or stores it through a broker, even when there’s no sale, gain, or change in ownership.

Critics have called it among the most punitive crypto taxes in the country, in part because Illinois imposes no comparable tax on dollars, gold, stocks or any other asset, and no other state has anything like it.

On Nov. 6, the Justice Department filed a statement of interest arguing the tax undermines federal policy and U.S. leadership in digital assets, calling it vague and discriminatory for singling out crypto for treatment no other asset faces.

The U.S. Chamber of Commerce and the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association, which represents hundreds of broker-dealers, banks and asset managers, filed an amicus brief warning the tax reaches well beyond crypto-native platforms to the traditional banks, custodians and advisers increasingly offering digital asset products.

A separate brief filed Nov. 11 brought together crypto’s heavy hitters, led by the DeFi Education Fund, Andreessen Horowitz, Paradigm and Uniswap, and including Aave Labs, Chainlink Labs, Solana Policy Institute, Phantom, Jito Labs, Multicoin Capital and Stand With Crypto.

Their filing argues the law’s definitions are so vague they could stretch to software developers and decentralized protocols that hold no customer funds and have no way to identify users or know where a transaction occurred. The brief noted an estimated 2.64 million Illinoisans hold digital assets, the fifth-highest total of any state.

“This tax is unclear, discriminatory, unconstitutional, and harmful to the future of finance,” CCI CEO Ji Hun Kim and Blockchain Association executive Ashok Pinto said in a joint statement.

The challenge dates to August. As Decrypt reported, Illinois agreed in October to pause the tax until July 1, 2027, buying time for the court fight now underway.

Microsoft Advertising Updates UI Following Advertiser Feedback via @sejournal, @brookeosmundson

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

Microsoft Advertising announces UI improvements addressing advertiser feedback, including easier navigation, expanded reporting, sign-in troubleshooting, and account access management.

Microsoft Advertising is updating its platform interface following advertiser feedback about navigation, reporting, and account access issues.

In its November 9 product newsletter, Microsoft acknowledged several problems advertisers have faced, including trouble finding reports and resolving sign-in issues.

The updates include easier navigation, expanded campaign reporting, clearer account permissions, and better sign-in troubleshooting.

Microsoft says more improvements are planned as part of its ongoing interface updates.

Microsoft is making changes to how advertisers move through the platform and review campaign performance.

The updated navigation menu groups features by common tasks and makes more options available by default. New breadcrumbs show where users are within their account and campaign settings.

Advertisers can also use navigation search and customize menus to reach frequently used features faster.

Microsoft acknowledged that its previous reporting tools made it difficult to find, compare, and export certain data.

The bidding updates could help advertisers understand performance changes, especially when automated bidding targets have changed over time.

Microsoft is also responding to feedback about account access problems, including issues that have prevented some advertisers from signing in.

The newsletter specifically mentions blank pages, unexpected redirects, and repeated sign-in attempts.

To help resolve these issues, Microsoft is adding clearer error messages, browser troubleshooting tips, and more specific recovery steps.

Microsoft is also working to make sign-ins more persistent, although it hasn’t shared details about how login behavior will change.

Account permissions and user management are getting updates as well.

Advertisers will see clearer information about user roles, permissions, and who can grant access to an account.

Invitation pages will provide more details about required permissions, expiration dates, and next steps. Administrators will also have better visibility into pending and expired invitations.

These changes could be helpful for agencies and in-house teams managing access across several accounts, especially when adding new users.

Microsoft also acknowledged feedback that some account recommendations have been too generic.

It’s working to improve when recommendations appear and explain why a specific change is being suggested for an account.

Some of these updates address common frustrations with Microsoft Advertising, while others provide more detail for reviewing campaign performance.

The bidding reports are one of the more useful additions, especially for advertisers using Target CPA or Target ROAS.

Seeing past targets alongside actual performance and conversion-delay data could help explain changes that aren’t obvious in standard campaign reports.

The search term reporting improvements should also provide more insight into how AI Max campaigns match queries with landing pages.

On the account management side, clearer sign-in instructions and permission settings could reduce the time spent troubleshooting access issues.

Microsoft reiterated that some of these features continue to rollout, so it’s possible not every advertiser has seen the updates in their account UI.

For advertisers who have struggled with reporting or account access, these changes address some familiar problems. How well the new experience works will become clearer as more advertisers gain access to the updates.

Zcash loses $1,000 as dormant wallets send ZEC coins to exchanges

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kairos Research found that traders exiting bullish positions drove the initial selloff, with fresh token deposits raising the risk of further losses.

Zcash fell below $1,000 as leveraged traders abandoned bullish positions and selling pressure intensified.

Data from CoinMarketCap showed ZEC at approximately $965 on Nov. 12, after a 24-hour low near $953.

The decline has exposed vulnerabilities in ZEC's market structure, with research showing heavy selling across derivatives exchanges and fresh deposits from previously inactive wallets.

A market investigation by Kairos Research identified the rapid closure of a 12,500 ZEC long position on Hyperliquid as a pivotal moment in the selloff.

The position, accumulated in the hour following the release of US inflation data, was closed through market orders within two seconds at 15:29:58 UTC on Nov. 10, just as Bitcoin began retreating.

ZEC subsequently fell from $1,193 to $1,001 as traders sold a net 160,000 ZEC across four perpetual futures exchanges over the following 90 minutes.

Binance spot hourly-closing price represented in the Kairos Research graphic. All readings are approximate, digitized from the line and rounded to the nearest $10; the underlying price observations were not available. The sharp decline occurred Nov. 10, 15:00-17:00 UTC. Price is separate because the import format has no dual axis.

Source: Kairos Research, ZEC market structure and exchange deposits (Nov. 11, 2026) · Kairos Research post, accessible mirror

Binance accounted for 74,000 ZEC, followed by OKX with 42,000, Hyperliquid with 31,000, and Bybit with 13,000. Spot markets recorded approximately 27,000 ZEC in net taker selling.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Kairos found that traders primarily drove the selling by unwinding existing bullish positions rather than forced liquidations.

On Hyperliquid, taker sales associated with closing longs reached 109,000 ZEC between 14:30 and 17:00 UTC, compared with 50,000 ZEC from traders opening shorts. Forced liquidations accounted for only 6,900 ZEC during that period.

Coinbase had already recorded approximately 3,000 ZEC in net spot selling before the breakdown, while derivatives traders on Binance, OKX and Hyperliquid remained net buyers.

The researchers also traced Coinbase deposits and found that only about 12,000 ZEC had arrived across Nov. 9 and 10, suggesting most of the initial spot selling came from inventory already held on the exchange.

However, fresh deposits accelerated after the decline.

By 18:00 UTC on Nov. 11, Coinbase had received another 16,300 ZEC, including 9,800 ZEC from three wallets that had been inactive since withdrawing funds from Coinbase Prime in November and December 2025.

Those wallets still held approximately 19,000 ZEC, while Binance received another 8,600 ZEC in large deposits over the same period.

The combined 24,900 ZEC in new exchange deposits raises the prospect of additional spot selling, although transfers alone do not establish an intention to liquidate.

Kairos Research image labels show approximate combined daily deposits to Coinbase, Coinbase Prime and Binance, including the separately marked Grayscale settlement. Amounts are rounded to the nearest 1,000 ZEC, not exact ledger totals. Nov. 11 is partial and covers deposits before 18:00 UTC. Exchange deposits do not establish sales.

Source: Kairos Research, ZEC market structure and exchange deposits (Nov. 11, 2026) · Kairos Research post, accessible mirror

The selling pressure has also extended to Grayscale's Zcash ETF, ZCSH, which is heading toward its second consecutive week of net withdrawals.

Data from SoSoValue showed that the fund recorded approximately $13 million in outflows during the week ending Nov. 6, followed by another $17 million this week.

The combined withdrawals are approaching $30 million.

The fund's cumulative net inflows have fallen to $192 million, while total net assets have also declined to $562 million from about $772 million at the end of the previous week.

However, Kairos found no evidence that fresh ETF redemptions caused the initial Nov. 10 selloff.

ZCSH traded 2.46 million shares that day without recording new creations or redemptions, while 1,861 ZEC transferred into Coinbase Prime represented settlement of an earlier withdrawal.

Despite the market downturn, Zcash continues to attract institutional backing amid growing interest in financial privacy, particularly as artificial intelligence expands the ability to analyze transactions on public blockchains.

Grayscale has emerged as a prominent advocate of this investment thesis, arguing that privacy could become an increasingly valuable feature of digital money.

In a research report, the asset manager said Zcash's monthly active developer population had roughly doubled over two years, while developer participation in the broader cryptocurrency industry has retreated from its previous peak.

The firm also found that approximately 70% of Zcash's active developers had more than one year of cryptocurrency development experience, compared with 58% across the industry.

Grayscale believes the expanding contributor base could improve wallet usability, network security and privacy infrastructure, eventually supporting higher ZEC valuations.

The bullish outlook also reflects progress in the network's development.

Zcash activated its NU7 upgrade on Nov. 6, reducing its target block interval from 75 seconds to 25 seconds and introducing changes designed to improve private payment processing.

Meanwhile, data from Zcash's shielded supply tracker showed that approximately 5 million ZEC, representing more than 29% of circulating supply, was held in shielded pools as of Nov. 10.

Grayscale has reinforced its bullish thesis with a $1 million commitment to the Zcash ecosystem, including $400,000 already awarded to four development and advocacy organizations.

The firm also launched a nationwide advertising campaign featuring a prominent Wall Street Journal promotion and a dedicated investment education platform.

It intends to reinvest sponsor fees collected from ZCSH into network development, marketing, and education for up to the fund's first year as an exchange-traded product.

Nonetheless, Grayscale's investments and Zcash's growing developer community have yet to reverse the deterioration in investor demand. With the fund approaching $30 million in withdrawals over two weeks and previously inactive wallets still holding another 19,000 ZEC, renewed selling could further test the cryptocurrency's ability to recover above $1,000.

Stock Market Midday, Nov. 12: Nvidia CEO Resigns, AMD Surges as Wall Street Edges Higher

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Stocks edged higher Thursday as strength in semiconductor shares helped support Wall Street, while gold prices tumbled.

Markets moved higher despite renewed uncertainty in the technology sector following the resignation of Nvidia (NVDA -5.29%) CEO Jensen Huang. As of 11:30 a.m. ET, the Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI +0.42%) led major indexes, gaining 0.29% to 52,641, while the S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.14%) advanced 0.19% to 7,634. The Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -0.03%) rose a modest 0.15% to 25,505 as investors weighed the implications of the leadership shakeup for the broader artificial intelligence industry.

Meanwhile, gold prices fell 3.05% to $3,878.30 as the benchmark 10-year Treasury yield climbed to 4.98%. Industrials and technology stocks led sector gains, while healthcare lagged, highlighting a mixed market despite the major indexes trading in positive territory.

Advanced Micro Devices (AMD +4.37%) surged 6.30% following an analyst upgrade, as investors assessed the potential competitive advantages arising from Nvidia's leadership turmoil. Huang's resignation, effective immediately, marks a significant turning point for the AI chipmaker, with longtime CFO Colette Kress stepping in as interim CEO.

Elsewhere, HubSpot (HUBS +6.96%) jumped 7.54% following a leadership overhaul aimed at strengthening its artificial intelligence strategy. Conversely, Super Micro Computer (SMCI -12.27%) tumbled 10.25%, while Alibaba (BABA -6.80%) fell 6.49% amid reports that U.S. regulators are considering additional restrictions on remote access to advanced AI chips.

Nvidia's leadership transition introduces a new layer of uncertainty into the artificial intelligence industry. Jensen Huang was instrumental in establishing Nvidia's dominance, and his departure could create opportunities for competitors like AMD to capture market share. However, Nvidia's entrenched software ecosystem, established customer relationships, and technological advantages remain formidable. Investors should watch whether the transition affects product development, customer spending, or the company's ability to maintain its competitive position heading into 2027.

Beyond the semiconductor industry, rising Treasury yields and persistent inflation remain important risks for equity investors. With the 10-year yield approaching 5%, higher borrowing costs could pressure corporate earnings and weigh on valuations, particularly among high-growth technology companies. The coming weeks will be critical as investors assess Nvidia's upcoming earnings, the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending, and whether economic conditions can support a broader year-end market rally.

Here's how you get to all three Sha-Chi Towers and Primal Stones for A Severed Mindseal in Phantom Blade

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Completing the Phantom Blade Zero A Severed Mindseal quest takes a long time as you've got to help the friendly Mandate Shadow Warden at three Sha-Chi Towers scattered across the game. The Weeping Creek Valley tower is part of the main story but the other two are optional: the Pangzhen Tower, which is confusingly actually in Meigu Lake, and the Crimson Forest Tower, which can't be accessed unless you've sufficiently progressed the main quest and a particular side quest – in short, if you've just started this quest, you don't need to worry about the other towers for ages.Specifically, you've got to find a Primal Stone for the warden to chow down on at each tower, which involves unique exploration efforts for each one. So, to make this quest as simple for you as possible, I've explained every step of A Severed Mindseal below, including how to get to each Sha-Chi Tower and find all the Primal Stones.

The Primal Stone is on the first floor of the tower around the back of the central furnace, but you need the Sha-Chi Furnace Inspection Crank to collect it:

To reach the Friendly Shadow Warden at the top of the Pangzhen/Meigu Lake Sha-Chi Tower, you need to run along the Pangzhen docks in the northeast of the city. Keep going along the wooden jetties until the walkway structure starts climbing up the cliff. Follow it all the way to the top and you'll get to a rope bridge and the Pangzhen Sha-Chi Tower Upper Level 1 Bell. This leads to the tower's south entrance and the start of your ascent.

However, the Primal Stone is in the lower section of the tower, which can only be reached by boat. Get the Sha-Chi-powered boat from the Pangzhen docks and sail around to the north side of the tower. Follow the red lanterns and you'll end up going down a channel that leads to the lower portion of the tower, then proceed as follows:

You can't reach this Sha-Chi Tower until you've reached the Mirage Estate in Pangzhen and have found all three Aberrant Three-Faced Puppet Memory Flowers within to trigger the next step of the Phantom Blade Zero Triple Threat quest. Only then can you fast travel to the now frozen Mirrorwood area of the forest where the Sha-Chi Tower is:

There unfortunately aren't any specific rewards for completing this quest, but now that you have the tower key, you can unlock that door near the lift and plunder the rest of the tower. When you get to the roof, you'll bump into some familiar faces and can take on the Phantom Blade Zero Heart of the Fist quest and get a few crucial Soul Tempering materials for some of great weapons.And this also isn't the last you'll see of Spectral Blade. I won't spoil what happens but, now that you've completed his quest, you'll find him in Soulrend Cliff where he will also give you a Soul Tempering material for a weapon. The Jensen Huang scandal is hardly over

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

A second affair has hit the news cycle, but that’s not what matters here.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

A second affair has hit the news cycle, but that’s not what matters here.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

I was certain, until the story of Jensen Huang’s second affair hit, that Huang was bulletproof. After all, why wouldn’t he be? He’s placed himself indispensably at the center of the AI industry and famously had no succession plan at Nvidia. In fact, he’s the only CEO Nvidia has ever had. L’état c’est moi.

Then two stories were published nearly back to back on Wednesday: one alleging Huang had an affair with his subordinate Kimberly Powell, another that the board was meeting to consider his ouster. First thing Thursday morning, Nvidia announced Huang had resigned. One of the most powerful men in Silicon Valley was out.

Why throw Huang under the bus for getting his nuts drained?

Fascinating. First of all, when I asked on Monday “Who else is Jensen Huang fucking?” I did not expect to get answers this fast — somebody, potentially somebody on the board (as we’ve seen in other incidents), wanted the story out there. Second, why throw Huang under the bus for getting his nuts drained? This is not an industry that cares about that kind of thing — just ask Eric Schmidt and Bill Gates. My guess is that the second affair is a pretext for action, and this all goes back to the original story from last week: that Huang was honeypotted by an alleged Chinese spy. Whatever was going on with neocloud Megaspeed might be much, much worse than we know, and what we know is pretty bad: business listings that led to “a Malaysian data center and shopping mall, a near-empty office in Singapore and a dilapidated storefront outside Kuala Lumpur.”

Even months before the report that Alice “No Relation” Huang, an exec at Megaspeed, was having an affair with Huang, there were questions about Nvidia’s business in China. The US government has placed strict export controls on Nvidia’s high-end chips. Huang claimed in May that as a result, Nvidia has zero percent market share in China. But also in May, short sellers Culper Research published a report in which they wrote, “We believe that in reality, over 20% of Nvidia’s FY 2026 compute revenues remained driven by China – supported both by illegal GPU diversion and Southeast Asian intermediaries.”

Megaspeed was Nvidia’s largest Southeast Asian buyer, but Singaporean newspaper The Straits Times visited Megaspeed’s Singapore office a month ago to find “no signs of activity.” Another building tenant told the reporters, “It is always dark for as long as it has been here. I hardly see anyone visiting or in the office.” The sole director of Megaspeed, Tan Yong Pong, appears to have vanished. The Singaporean company that owns Megaspeed, Swiftdata, has listed an office that belongs to an interior design firm as its business address.

Incidentally, Alice Huang has surfaced in Beijing. The Financial Times says she’s barred from Singapore for giving police “inaccurate information.” Oh, and reportedly her companies invested in Chinese AI company DeepSeek and promised them Nvidia’s Blackwell chips? Fun!

Megaspeed is only part of the story, I think. There are two Chinese chip-related scandals that could cause serious problems for Nvidia, and both feature people with close ties to Huang. One is Megaspeed, which involves Huang’s maybe-spy affair partner. The other centers on Super Micro (which, horribly, does business as Supermicro), a partner of Nvidia run by one of Huang’s old friends, Charles Liang. Super Micro’s cofounder, Yih-Shyan “Wally” Liaw, along with Ruei-Tsang “Steven” Chang and Ting-Wei “Willy” Sun, allegedly defrauded the US by serving as a passthrough for a front company to buy $2.5 billion of Nvidia chips for Alibaba. The front company, not named in the indictment, is Thailand-based OBON, according to Bloomberg. Liang and Super Micro have not been charged.

But these incidents may not be the end of Nvidia’s problems. Take for instance, more recently, New Matrix — another company that barely exists, and yet is an approved buyer of Nvidia chips. It is, in fact, one of several approved companies that “have ties to firms that have been blacklisted by the U.S. government, or that work as military contractors in China,” according to The New York Times.

The question, of course, is how high this all goes

Huang has insisted that Nvidia has seen “no evidence of any AI chip diversion.” He’s been pretty emphatic on that point, actually. I am now wondering if that is even a little bit true. If the board is cutting Huang loose, there’s probably more on chip smuggling allegations coming.

More substantively, Nvidia reportedly intervened in a 2025 Commerce Department investigation of Megaspeed, which was suspected of smuggling Nvidia chips — halting the investigation. Since the scandal broke, Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick appears to have become the fall guy for this, with Axios reporting that Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent “confronted” him about failing to note Megaspeed as a national security concern and Politico saying “more than half a dozen Republicans have demanded answers” from him.

The question, of course, is how high this all goes. After all, Huang was awfully buddy-buddy with President Donald Trump, who was informed about Huang’s maybe-spy girlfriend in 2025 and apparently did nothing about it. Incidentally, Nvidia has reportedly donated $25 million to Trump’s planned ballroom. This is what gangster tech regulation looks like.

I wonder what else was an open secret at the company, you know?

Come January, Elizabeth Warren will be able to send subpoenas in her role as the chair of the Senate Banking Committee. And wouldn’t you know, she seems to be pretty interested. Democrats will control both the House and the Senate. Their voters have given them a mandate to root out corruption. And boy howdy, does Nvidia look like a sitting duck right now. If Huang knew chips were being diverted — or had reason to know that — and tried to ensure their sales anyway, he may have committed actual crimes (bribery, espionage, smuggling, whatever) and, under a functioning legal system, could actually go to jail. Nvidia dispensing with its indispensable CEO is the company reducing its risk of getting roped into Huang’s drama.

Will it be that easy for Nvidia? I wonder. The most interesting part of the second affair story, with Powell, the Nvidia vice president of healthcare, comes at the very end of the New York Times article. When someone took a video of Powell and Huang being “affectionate” in March, Huang’s security had nondisclosure agreements ready to go. CNBC reports that rumors of their affair have circulated “for years.” I wonder what else was an open secret at the company, you know? Especially since Huang had 60 direct reports, so every part of the company is covered in his fingerprints.

Over in the tech world, people still seem to be in denial. On X, the social network for Trumpist dead-enders, the account “zerohedge” posted, “Elon please hire Jensen.” Elon Musk replied, “That would be an honor.”
