Wipfli: Leaders must rethink risk management as AI, cyber and operational risks converge

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Wipfli advisors share why enterprise risk visibility, governance and a strategy-first approach are becoming critical drivers of growth and resilience, not just protection.

MILWAUKEE, Nov. 12, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- As organizations accelerate AI adoption, navigate rising cybersecurity threats and pursue growth initiatives, many are discovering that risk can no longer be managed in silos. According to insights shared during a recent Wipfli leadership panel, resilience today depends on connecting operational, technology, cybersecurity and data risks across the enterprise.

"The goal isn't to eliminate risk, it's to understand it and decide what's acceptable and move forward with confidence," said Carrie Connell, partner in Wipfli's risk advisory practice.

Resilience starts with understanding critical services

To put that approach into practice, leaders can map a critical business service end-to-end to identify dependencies that could undermine resilience, slow decisions or impede growth. The exercise identifies who owns the service; which people, processes, technologies, vendors and data support it; where decisions escalate; and what must continue during a disruption.

That focus on keeping critical services running also requires preparation for cyber disruption. Jeff Olejnik, partner and cybersecurity leader at Wipfli, encouraged organizations to build and rehearse incident response plans before a crisis.

AI is creating new opportunities and new risks

Employees' independent use of AI tools is creating a governance challenge for organizations, panelists said. While AI can improve efficiency and support innovation, employees may use tools outside established approval processes and enter confidential or personally identifiable information without understanding how it will be handled. That disconnect can expose organizations to security, privacy and compliance risks.

Matt Sabo, director of Wipfli's AI Center of Excellence, emphasized defining the business outcome and how success will be measured before building an AI solution. "A successful AI implementation starts with a good business strategy." Panelists recommended measuring business outcomes rather than adoption alone, with approved AI tools and clear acceptable use policies providing the oversight needed to support innovation.

Waiting to modernize also carries risk, cautioned Tom Loring, a Wipfli partner specializing in IT auditing and information security. He encouraged leaders to weigh the risks of delay against their strategic goals and intended business outcomes, then use their risk tolerance to guide pilots or phased rollouts while maintaining foundational safeguards. "The strategy should drive the decisions about which risks the organization needs to manage."

AI also has a role in defending against cyberthreats, Olejnik emphasized. "AI needs to be used as part of your defense mechanism so that you can respond at machine speed and not at human speed."

Governance should enable faster decisions

Rather than adding layers of oversight, organizations should focus on improving decision-making clarity, according to the panel.

"The goal is not more governance for its own sake," Connell said. She emphasized that organizations already feeling the strain of additional oversight need clarity about who participates in decisions, what information matters, who has authority to act and when issues must be escalated. With those elements established, risk management can support faster decisions instead of adding friction.

For major initiatives, that clarity means aligning technology, risk, compliance and business operations around shared objectives, defined responsibilities and decision rights. Clear accountability connects those functions rather than leaving each to manage risk independently.

Practical steps organizations can take now

Panelists recommended four priorities for the next 90 days:

The goal is resilience that protects against disruption while enabling organizations to innovate, grow and respond confidently to change.

For a deeper look at identifying cross-functional risk, preparing for cyber disruption and aligning AI initiatives with business strategy, watch Wipfli's on-demand webinar, Balancing Risk, Resilience and Growth.

Intoxalock Rolls Out New One-Touch Breathalyzer Device in Pennsylvania and New Jersey

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Following a successful pilot in Pennsylvania in 2025, the simplified 2001A ignition interlock device pairs with the Intoxalock mobile app and offers increased ease of use, giving drivers access as the company evaluates wider availability.

PHILADELPHIA, Nov. 12, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Intoxalock, a Mindr brand and the leading ignition interlock device provider in the U.S., today announced the rollout of its new 2001A ignition interlock device across Pennsylvania and New Jersey following a successful 2025 pilot.

Intoxalock launches its new 2001A ignition interlock device in PA and NJ, featuring single-blow testing and app pairing.

The new 2001A breathalyzer device is designed to make daily testing simpler and more intuitive for drivers. Its features include a one-touch single start, single-blow pattern and clear visual feedback. When they pair the device with the Intoxalock mobile app, drivers can view their results, track their appointments and manage their account from their phone.

Key Features of the New Intoxalock Breathalyzer:

The pilot program began in Pennsylvania in 2025, where strong results and service center readiness supported a full rollout across both Pennsylvania and New Jersey. Feedback from drivers and service partners helped shape that expansion, and Intoxalock will continue evaluating performance in both states as it assesses wider availability of the 2001A.

"The rollout to Pennsylvania and New Jersey reflects our commitment to our customers and our broader mission to help people move forward responsibly after a drunk driving charge," said Kathy Boden Holland, CEO of Mindr. "After a successful pilot, we're excited to bring the new breathalyzer device to the market and continue to look at making it available in new states."

The feedback that the company has received has been positive. Many customers note that the device is easy to use and fits into their daily routines. For drivers balancing work, family and program requirements, the enhanced changes to the new device help make the process a little easier. Service centers note that the breathalyzer device makes it easier to walk drivers through the testing process after installation, so drivers leave the shop feeling confident and ready to use it on their own.

"My experience with the new device has been great," said an Intoxalock customer. "It doesn't give me any issues and the breath sample is easy. I also appreciated that Intoxalock followed up with me to make sure everything was going well and to answer my questions. I have felt supported throughout the whole process."

The 2001A is now available at participating Intoxalock service centers in Pennsylvania and New Jersey. Intoxalock continues to assess the availability of the new breathalyzer device in other states.

Intoxalock is the leading ignition interlock provider in the U.S., with the nation's largest service center network and a proven track record of helping people safely get back on the road. Intoxalock works with legal and state systems to promote responsible driving, offering trustworthy solutions for compliance and prevention.

Headquartered near Des Moines, Iowa, Intoxalock is owned and operated by Consumer Safety Technology, LLC and is part of the Mindr family of brands.

For more information, visit www.intoxalock.com.

Cuando un agente controla dispositivos fÃ­sicos, la fiabilidad es mÃ¡s importante que la inteligencia. Tuya Cloud Devices incorpora lÃ­mites de seguridad en cada etapa. CorMedix (CRMD) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Heron deal and DefenCath reimbursement shifts drive the quarter.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

CorMedix Inc. (CRMD -4.72%) reported third-quarter financial results highlighted by the announced acquisition of Heron Therapeutics assets and the strategic transition of DefenCath into a new CMS reimbursement framework. Management reported that the company is diversifying its portfolio to reduce reliance on its lead product, targeting pro forma 2027 revenue from the acquired Heron assets of $125 million to $150 million. The company confirmed its full-year 2026 revenue guidance while narrowing its adjusted EBITDA targets to the high end of previous estimates. Management indicated that they have suspended 2027 revenue guidance for DefenCath until they gain better visibility into the impact of the quarterly reimbursement reset and potential Medicare Advantage contracting.

Operator: Good morning, and welcome to the CorMedix Third Quarter 26 Earnings and Corporate Update Conference Call. Today's conference call is being recorded. There will be a question and answer session at the end of today's presentation. And instructions on how to ask a question will be given at that time. Before we begin, I would like to remind everyone that during the call, we may make what are known as forward looking statements within the meaning set forth in the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995.

These statements are statements other than statements of historical fact regarding management's expectations, beliefs, goals, and plans about the company's prospects and future financial position. Actual results may differ materially from the estimates and on which these statements are based due to a variety of important factors. Formedex does not intend to update these forward looking statements except as required by law.

This call, the company will discuss certain non GAAP measures of its performance. GAAP to non GAAP financial reconciliations and supple supplemental financial information provided in CorMedix earnings release and the current report on Form 8-K filed with the SEC. This information is also available on the Investor Relations section of CorMedix's website. In addition, today's discussion includes statements regarding CorMedix proposed acquisition of substantially all the assets of Heron Therapeutics, which remains subject to approval by Heron's stockholders. Regulatory clearance, and other customary closing conditions. Formax has filed a current report on Form 8-K describing the proposed transaction in greater detail. Investors are urged to read it.

And any other relevant documents CorMedix has filed with the SEC carefully and in their entirety because they contain important information about the proposed transaction. At this time, it is now my pleasure to turn the call over to Joe Todisco chairman and chief executive officer of CorMedix. Joe, please go ahead.

Joseph Todisco: Thanks, Daniel. Good morning, everyone. And thank you for joining us on this call. On Monday, we announced the definitive agreement to acquire substantially all of the assets of Heron Therapeutics. Encompassing both commercial and pipeline assets that we believe are highly complementary and synergistic with CorMedix growth strategy in institutional settings of care. As we discussed on Monday, we see 4 pillars to the strategic rationale for this transaction. First, scale and diversification. The Heron portfolio meaningfully expands our revenue base and further diversifies our revenue mix across acute care and hematology oncology supportive care and reduces our reliance on DefenCath revenues to drive cash flow and future growth.

Heron's portfolio generated approximately $150 million of net product revenue over the trailing 12 months, and we expect the acquired products to contribute approximately $125 million to $150 million of revenue for pro forma 2027. Roughly doubling the revenue base for our non-DEFENCATH portfolio. Second, commercial and operational leverage. We expect to deploy Heron's products through our existing infrastructure supplemented with a subset of the Heron team, which supports the 45 to $60 million in estimated operating cost synergies that we expect to achieve on an annualized run rate basis by the end of 27. Third, growth from the acute care franchise.

We have used ZYNRELEF in particular as an asset with significant untapped potential as hospitals and surgery centers increasingly adopt non opioid approaches to post surgical pain. And together with APONVIE, which serves the same perioperative call point represents the fastest growing parts of the Heron portfolio. And fourth, hematology oncology supported care and pipeline optionality. CINVANTI and SUSTOL both bring an established presence in infusion centers and hematology oncology clinics, which aligns directly with our planned launch of REZZAYO, for the prophylaxis indication. If approved by FDA. I also want to highlight how we structure this transaction to align payment with sales performance.

Up to $55 million of the consideration is contingent on ZYNRELEF achieving net sales milestones in calendar year 2028. And there are additional contingent payments in the form of a royalty above a sales threshold for CINVANTI, SUSTOL, and any products we launch under the multiproduct development agreement. To that extent, a meaningful portion of what we pay is earned only if the assets perform. We expect the transaction to be immediately accretive to earnings and free cash flow upon closing which we anticipate in the first quarter of 2027, subject to Heron stockholder approval, regulatory clearance and other customary closing conditions. Turning to third quarter performance. We continue to execute on our 3 core strategic priorities.

First, executing on our post-TDAPA commercial strategy for DefenCath. Second, meaningfully advancing our high value pipeline. Highlighted by FDA's acceptance of the sNDA for REZZAYO, the prophylaxis of invasive fungal disease with priority review. And third, deploying our capital in a disciplined manner to fuel sustainable growth highlighted by the Heron announcement. We believe this combination of commercial execution, pipeline progression and strategic capital allocation positions CorMedix to drive long term value to shareholders building an increasingly diversified and resilient business. For the third quarter, we reported consolidated revenue of $54.5 million above consensus and adjusted EBITDA of $3.1 million and we ended the quarter with $355 million in cash.

Importantly, our largest dialysis customers largely maintained DefenCath utilization through the quarter at the $2.37 add on in effect for the second half of 26. Susan will provide more granular details of third quarter financial results. Turning to guidance. Last week, we reaffirmed our full-year 2026 revenue guidance with a range of $325 million to $345 million And today, we are also narrowing our full year adjusted EBITDA guidance to the top end of the previous range. To 130 million to $140 million. We also issued a business update last week addressing CMS' release of the ESRD PTS final rule for 2020.

Well as comments made by the CFO of a large dialysis organization related to the 2027 post-TDAPA add-on payment for Defencast. Well, the ESRD final rule established the post-TDAPA add-on of $8.30 for the first quarter of 2027, which is within our previously guided range, CMS also finalized the new methodology to calculate add on payments whereby they will be reset quarterly. Because the quarterly add on incorporates recent utilization and pricing data, its trajectory quarter to quarter depends on information that only CorMedix can report to CMS. We have not shared that data with any customer or third party, we believe third party projections of the quarterly path are subject to significant uncertainty.

As we disclosed in our announcement, this new quarterly mechanism will result in overall average reimbursement to customers that is lower than the $8.32 over the course of the year. However, based on our view of our own pricing utilization data, we currently expect to add on in each quarter of 2027 to exceed the current $2.37. And we currently estimate the full year average to be at least double that current rate. After a decline from Q1 to Q2, we expect the add on in Q3 and or Q4 of 27 to be higher than the second quarter. In each case, subject to how CMS applies this methodology, including certain utilization and pricing data in its calculations.

Put simply, the lowest add on we currently expect DefenCath to see in 2027 is higher than our current rate customers received today. And our LDO and MDO customers have largely maintained utilization at today's current rate in the third quarter. We are currently engaged in conversations with customers around how to structure DefenCath pricing that tracks the quarterly add on adjustments with the goal of keeping fee for service patients on therapy throughout the year. We are also actively working to secure separate payment for DefenCath from top Medicare Advantage plan.

As a result, we will not be in a position to opine on any 2027 revenue guidance for DefenCath until we have visibility into both customer pricing and utilization for Medicare fee for service patients as well as possible upside from Medicare Advantage. In that regard, we are deferring 2027 DefenCath guidance at this time and anticipate being able to update investors in early 27. We continue to believe that Medicare Advantage represents a meaningful long term growth avenue for DefendCast and the best opportunity for the product to have sustainable value following the ending of the post-TDAPA add on in 2029.

DefenCath has clinical value and its potential for meaningful downstream cost savings continue to be supported by a growing body of real world evidence that our partners are publishing and I am proud of the data presentation that took place this past October at the American Society of Nephrology's Kidney Week at IDWeek. Turning to our pipeline. We announced in October that the sNDA for REZZAYO in prophylaxis was accepted for FDA review with priority review status and a target action date from the FDA in the first quarter of 2027. As a reminder, submission was conducted by our global partner, Mundipharma, following positive clinical trial results from the ReSPECT study.

Phase 3 clinical study evaluating REZZAYO for the prophylaxis of invasive fungal disease in adult immunosuppressed patients undergoing allogeneic bone and marrow transplant. We are actively preparing for promotional efforts for REZZAYO in this new indication And if approved by FDA, anticipate commencement of promotional efforts in the second half of 27. Finally, with respect to our Phase III TPN study for DefenCath, despite engagement of new study sites and the FDA-approved protocol amendment that narrows certain exclusion criteria, Study enrollment continues to track meaningfully behind our original estimated time lines. Given the established safety profile for DefenCath and demonstrated efficacy in currently approved indication.

We are evaluating potential alternative pathways to pursue approval with FDA should enrollment not accelerate in the next 1 to 2 months. We expect to make a decision on the strategy for TPN prior to our next quarterly update call. I would like to now turn the call over to Susan to discuss the company's third quarter financial results and financial position. Susan?

Susan Blum: You, Joe, and good morning, everyone. As a reminder, the Melinta acquisition closed on August 29, 2025. As a result, the third quarter of 2026 includes a full quarter of Melinta operations compared with the third quarter of 2025, which only has 1 month of Melinta's operations. This difference affects the year over year comparison of both revenue and expenses in a meaningful way. We also filed our Form 10 Q this morning and I encourage investors to review it for additional information regarding our third quarter and year to date results. Financial position and related disclosures.

Overall, our third quarter results reflect the anticipated transition of DefenCath into the post-TDAPA reimbursement environment, continued contributions from the broader product portfolio and disciplined management of our operating expense base. Now turning to the drivers. Consolidated revenue for the third quarter of 2026 was $54.5 million compared with $104.3 million in the third quarter of 2025. Third quarter consolidated revenue consisted of $17.2 million in DefenCath net sales and $37.3 million from the remainder of the portfolio, consisting of $31.4 million of product sales, and approximately $5.9 million of contract revenue and grant income under our BARDA agreement.

The year over year decline in DefenCath revenue primarily reflects the lower net pricing associated with its transition on July 1, to the post-TDAPA reimbursement period. Importantly, large outpatient dialysis customers largely maintained utilization during the quarter despite the reimbursement change. DefenCath net revenue included a $5 million favorable change in estimate related to certain Medicaid rebate and product return allowances. The adjustment reflects updated information regarding Medicaid utilization as well as recent DefendCast product return experience. Both of which have been much lower than previously estimated. Total operating expenses were $36.6 million in the third quarter of 2026, compared with $41.7 million in the third quarter of 2025. A decrease of approximately 12%.

The reduction primarily reflects merger related and reorganization costs incurred following the Melinta acquisition in the prior year period, which was partially offset by the inclusion of a full quarter of costs associated with the expanded organization in 2026 versus just 1 month of Melinta's costs in the third quarter of 2025. R&D expenses were $7 million in the quarter, compared with $5.1 million in the third quarter of 2025. The increase was primarily attributable to higher personnel and clinical trial costs supporting our combined company ongoing development programs. Selling and marketing expenses were $11.7 million in the quarter, compared with $11.2 million in the prior year quarter.

The modest increase reflects personnel marketing investments supporting the expanded product portfolio partially offset by the cost savings associated with the elimination of overlapping commercial teams that were present for a portion of the third quarter of 2025. G&A expenses decreased to $17.9 million from $25.5 million in the third quarter of 2025. The decrease year over year was largely attributable to transaction integration, and reorganization expenses incurred in connection with the Melinta acquisition during the prior year period and partially offset by higher costs in 2026 of operating the combined company. Including investments in personnel, IT, legal support, and facilities.

On the bottom line, on a GAAP basis, CorMedix reported a net loss of $14.7 million or $0.19 per basic and diluted share compared with net income of $108.6 million or $1.42 per basic share and $1.26 per diluted share in the third quarter of 2025. A large driver of net income in the prior year period was a substantial tax benefit of $59.7 million associated with the partial reversal of the valuation allowance on deferred tax assets. Third quarter 2026 GAAP results included an $11.5 million noncash expense from the quarterly remeasurement of contingent consideration associated with the Melinta acquisition.

This expense reflected changes in the discount rates applied to expected milestone and royalty payments as well as accretion of the liability from the passage of time. On a non GAAP basis, adjusted EBITDA was $3.1 million for the third quarter of 2026 compared with $71.9 million in the third quarter of 2025. Adjusted EBITDA excludes depreciation and amortization, stock based compensation, interest and other income and expense, changes in contingent consideration, taxes, merger related costs, and other specified adjustments. The third quarter of 2026, the calculation also excludes the $5 million favorable change in estimate related to sales allowances reported during the quarter. We ended the quarter with $355.2 million in cash and cash equivalents.

For the first 9 months of 2026, net cash provided by operating activities was $218.3 million compared with $80.6 million in the first 9 months of 2025. The year over year increase in cash flow reflects the higher year to date earnings achieved during the current year relative to 2025, as well as working capital movements, including the timing of certain contractual rebate payments. As discussed earlier, we are maintaining our full-year 2026 consolidated revenue guidance of $325 million to $345 million and we are narrowing our adjusted EBITDA guidance to $130 million to $140 million the high end of our previous range.

We have also lowered and narrowed our full year operating expense guidance which excludes noncash items such as stock based compensation, up to $140 million to $145 million. With the integration of Melinta behind us, and the financial flexibility afforded to us, we believe we are well positioned to drive significant value for shareholders in the many ways that Joe has mentioned. And now I will turn the call back to Joe for closing remarks. Joe?

Joseph Todisco: Thanks, Susan. I am excited about the potential for the organization. CorMedix is building a leading diversified commercial platform across institutional settings of care. Altimmune (ALT) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Spending is rising fast as the pemvidutide Phase 3 programs move forward.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Altimmune, Inc. (ALT -8.81%) reported a net loss for the third quarter as it increased research and development spending to support its late-stage clinical pipeline for pemvidutide. Management stated that the company is executing a strategy to build a comprehensive liver disease franchise targeting metabolic dysfunction-associated steatohepatitis and alcohol-related conditions. The company reported that its current cash and investment balance of $480 million is expected to provide funding through the anticipated 52-week data readout for its global Phase 3 MASH trial in 2029. Management indicated that it is evaluating nondilutive funding options and potential strategic partnerships to support the advancement of its alcohol use disorder program following upcoming regulatory discussions.

Jerome Benedict Durso: Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us today. We enter the final months of the year with strong confidence in pemvidutide, and the strategy we are laser-focused on executing. Over the course of this year, we significantly advanced our programs across the board in NASH, AUD, and ALD, further strengthening our belief that pemvidutide's differentiated clinical profile can support a broad liver franchise with multiple indications in serious liver diseases. We are now focused on carrying that momentum forward through disciplined execution. We continue to see a growing understanding and scientific support for the potential therapeutic role of PEMVY and its ability to provide meaningful benefit to people affected by serious liver diseases.

MASH data presented at several podium talks at the meeting reinforced the potential of the dual glucagon GLP-1 mechanism in MASH. And underscored the differentiation of pemvidutide. We presented new NIT data from our 48-week impact study that highlighted the strong antifibrotic activity of PEMV that continues to compare favorably to data across other trials. We believe pemvidutide's balanced 1-to-1 glucagon to GLP-1 ratio when coupled with our proprietary UPORT domain matters. And results in a molecule that balances metabolic and liver directed activity generally favorable tolerability.

Despite new products entering the NASH field, and others in various stages of development, there remains a significant unmet need for patients. And we believe that pemvidutide could represent a well differentiated MASH therapy. We see a major commercial opportunity across the indications we are targeting as we build our liver franchise. In addition to the unmet need in NASH, we view AUD and ALD as part of a patient continuum as AUD patients often progress to liver impairment. In fact, data referenced at the liver meeting estimates that 55% to 94% of ALD patients also meet the criteria for AUD.

The RECLAIM data highlighted that PEMI has the potential to help address both alcohol consumption and the liver damage that can result from it. Finally, 1 of the recurring themes at the liver meeting was about the overlap of NASH and alcohol use. We believe pemvidutide's differentiated profile positions it well across a multitude patients. Moving now on to execution, which is a critical focus for Altimmune. First, our global PERFORMA Phase III trial is ramping up, and we are very encouraged by the progress to date. Clinical trial sites continue to come online according to our plan, and we are actively enrolling patients. The engagement we see from the PERFORMA investigators and the broader NASH community is strong.

Excitement was quite evident at the liver meeting. Christophe will touch on the positive feedback and engagement from KOLs on Performa and pemvidutide overall. We are also pleased that the IMPACT Phase 2b 48-week efficacy and safety data was published in The Lancet this week. In AUD, the positive RECLAIM Phase II data were highlighted in a late breaking oral presentation. We believe this is the most robust AUD data presented to date. The strong results we saw significantly derisk this program. Importantly, our end of phase 2 meeting with the FDA is scheduled for next month. Representing a key step towards advancing pemvidutide in AUD.

We look forward to engaging with the agency and continuing our progress of PEMVY in the indication. We As part of our continued evolution as a late stage biotech company, we are also thrilled to recently have added Peter Greenleaf, and Alex Horvath to our Board of Directors. Peter and Alex are accomplished industry leaders that bring a breadth of experience across drug development, commercialization, and corporate development. Our conviction in pemvidutide is stronger than ever and we look forward to sharing our continued progress. I will now turn the call over to Christophe for a clinical update.

Christophe Arbet-Engels: Thank you, Jerry. I would like to start by just stating that I am very pleased with the significant progress we are making as we advance our clinical programs across NASH, AUD and ALD. Beginning with MASH, the global PERFORMA phase 3 trial is underway and progressing as planned. We are screening and dosing patients and clinical site activation continues across the program as we build towards broader global enrollment. We have the majority of planned US sites now active, and recruiting patients, and we expect to activate the ex US sites over the coming months. We will continue to work closely with our CRO partner to support efficient trial execution and enrollment.

Overall, we remain encouraged by the enthusiasm and engagement we are seeing from investigators and clinical sites for PERFORMA based on the product profile and the efficient trial design. As a reminder, we anticipate the PERFORMA 52-week data readout in 2029. Altimmune had a strong presence at the recent Liver Meeting in Denver, where we had the opportunity to highlight the potential of pemvidutide in serious liver diseases. We saw a growing interest and excitement for pemvidutide from the scientific community and the role it may play in treating the liver damage that characterizes the overlapping metabolic and alcohol associated diseases of NASH, AUD, and ALD.

During the liver meeting, we presented 2 analyses from the IMPACT Phase IIb trial including compelling data showing concurrent improvement across multiple non invasive measures of liver fat inflammation and fibrosis compared to patients receiving placebo. The new data further illustrates the strong and consistent antifibrotic activity and a generally favorable tolerability profile in the absence of dose titration. Our data along with emerging data from other glucagon GLP-1 programs reinforce the importance of glucagon in treating NASH. The totality of our learning during the liver meeting further support our Phase 3 study design. We also hosted well attended disease state product theater where Dr. Mazen Noureddin, Dr. Rohit Loomba and Dr. Jessica Mellinger discuss the advanced liver disease continuum.

They noted the prognostic components of fibrosis on outcomes and how various factors, including alcohol consumption or metabolic dysfunction, impact the progression of serious liver disease. The product theater further underscored the overlap between excessive alcohol consumption and metabolic causes of serious liver disease, and the high unmet need for therapy that can chronically address both causes and their liver damage consequences. We believe that the data presented on PEMVY during the liver meetings strongly support the potential of pemvidutide to address that unmet need with a generally well tolerated approach that is particularly suitable for chronic diseases such as MASH, AUD, and ALD in addition, Dr.

Henry Kranzler, the lead investigator for our RECLAIM Phase 2 trial presented a very robust and positive study results in the late breaking oral presentation at the liver meeting. To our knowledge, the reclaim data is the strongest data in AUD to date. The trial showed statistically significant improvements with pemvidutide across the primary endpoint of heavy drinking days, as well as key secondary measures including WHO risk drinking level achievements of 0 heavy drinking days both of which are FDA registrational endpoints. Importantly, we also demonstrated that PEST an objective serum based measure of alcohol consumption, corroborated the self reported reduction in alcohol intake in pemvidutide-treated patients. Pemvidutide also increased abstinence in patients with AUD.

And while RECLAIM was focused on drinking behaviors, the trial demonstrated in an exploratory analysis that PEMVY decreased the proportion of patients with elevated risk for developing advanced liver fibrosis as measured by FIB-4. These data point towards potential liver improvement in this patient population. Given PEMV's direct glucagon effect on the liver, the observed data is very encouraging. And we expect to further evaluate the potential liver benefits in the registrational Phase III study in AUD with inclusion of a subpopulation of AUD patients with advanced liver impairment.

Importantly, RECLAIM was conducted in patients with moderate to severe AUD the population we believe may have the greatest need for more effective and safe treatment options and who are at risk of alcohol related liver disease. The overall strong positive results including the generally favorable safety profile observed in the trial positions us well to engaging with regulatory agencies to align on the next steps for the program. To that end, our end of phase 2 meeting with the FDA is scheduled for December. Where we expect to discuss the study design and regulatory path for a Phase 3 AUD program. Typically, meeting minutes follow within 30 days.

In our registrational program, we will focus on moderate to severe patients, including a subpopulation of patients with advanced liver impairments such as ALD. This approach not only aligns with FDA guidance, where patients with hepatic impairment should be enrolled in AUD registration program, but also may address the current unmet need as therapies for AUD are not specifically indicated in the hepatically impaired subpopulation. In terms of trial design, we expect the primary endpoint to be the WHO RDL with no heavy drinking days as a key secondary endpoint both of which reached statistical significance in the RECLAIM Phase 2 trial. We expect other secondary exploratory endpoints to include additional self reported drinking endpoints.

PET, body weight, and liver endpoints, liver fat content, liver stiffness, measurements, and health. We would look to include both 1.8 milligram and 4 mg doses with a simple 1 to 2 step titration. The PROCURE trial will have a primary efficacy read at 26 weeks, and continue up to 1 year for additional safety and efficacy data. The final program design will of course be subject to our dialogue with regulators. Moving now to ALD. The RESTORE trial enrolled 121 patients with ALD and a history of chronic and heavy drinking at baseline. The 48-week trial is designed to demonstrate the liver benefit over a period of 48 weeks, and includes additional evaluations at 24 weeks.

Given the significant overlap between the ALD and AUD patient population, demonstrating liver benefits in the RESTORE trial could further support a unique role for pemvidutide in decreasing both chronic alcohol consumption and the liver damage it causes. We also believe that the ALD trial will further strengthen our filing package and discussion with regulators when seeking an AUD indication. We expect the top line data for RESTORE in the second half of 27. In summary, the significant progress achieved during 2026 across all our clinical program positions, Altimmune well heading into 2027. I echo Jerry's sentiment that our conviction in pemiglitide is stronger than ever.

We look forward to executing against the key clinical and regulatory milestones ahead moving us closer to bringing pemvidutide to patients. And with that, I will turn the call over to Linda.

Linda Richardson: Thanks, Christophe. Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us today. Following the conclusion of the 26 liver meeting, I would like to reflect on why we believe pemvidutide remains well positioned in the NASH and AUD markets as a differentiated potential future therapy. First, new data presented at the liver meeting clearly supports the validity of the dual glucagon GLP-1 mechanism of action in the treatment of NASH. That said, the molecule matters as we see different profiles emerging within the glucagon GLP-1 class. Pemvy's balanced 1-to-1 glucagon GLP-1 ratio has already shown significant early improvements in NASH resolution in our Phase 2b IMPACT study. And strong data on fibrosis improvements based on 24 and 48-week NIT analyses.

In addition to the strong profile of the 1.8 milligram dose we saw in phase 2, the 2.4 milligram dose now being studied in our 52-week PERFORMA Phase 3 MASH trial may offer additional upside in terms of weight loss and efficacy. Our generally favorable tolerability profile seen now across multiple pemvidutide trials, and lack of significant drug interactions, are important product features that should help patients remain on PEMVY therapy as well as continue medications they may be taking for comorbid conditions.

These 2 specific features, in addition to our dual mechanism of action, suggest that PEMVY may play an important future role as a potential combination therapy for NASH patients who do not reach therapeutic goals or cannot tolerate their current therapy. Second, despite the availability of approved therapies in MASH, 1 thing remains clear. A significant unmet need remains. While encouraged by the growth seen to date in the MASH market, diagnosis and treatment rates remain low. But we expect this dynamic to evolve. We also believe that evaluating MASH treatment options for patients will move beyond focusing primarily on diabetes and obesity comorbidities to also address additional needs.

For example, patients diagnosed with NASH who also drink, those who are at risk for sarcopenia and need to preserve lean muscle mass, and postmenopausal women facing decreases in bone mineral density may be important patient segments for pemvidutide in the future. Third, with Pemvy, we have a significant opportunity to potentially redefine what to expect from drug therapy and alcohol use disorder. Based on our specific profile of reducing alcohol consumption, and perhaps demonstrating direct improvements on the liver given our glucagon activity. This could subsequently help activate 1 of the largest underserved markets in The US. As a reminder, approximately 27 million Americans meet the criteria for AUD.

And 12 million of these patients are believed to have moderate to severe AUD. This more advanced segment has the highest risk of progressing to severe liver damage and is where pemvidutide's dual mechanism of action may be most compelling. Christophe highlighted our AUD data from RECLAIM earlier in the call. But I would like to also note that our early mover status in AUD is a competitive advantage for PEMVY as we are generating important new data in this field.

Pemvy's balanced 1-to-1 glucagon GLP-1 ratio providing both metabolic and direct liver benefits generally favorable tolerability profile, lack of significant drug interactions, and simple patient friendly dosing drives significant differentiation versus other therapies in development across NASH, AUD, and ALD. We are excited about PEMVY's potential, confident in our existing data and look forward to sharing additional insights on future calls. Gregory, you will now share our financials for the third quarter.

Suburban Propane Partners, L.P. (SPH -1.48%) reported an increase in adjusted EBITDA for fiscal 2026, supported by expansion in its renewable natural gas platform and stable results in core propane operations despite regional weather variability. Management reported that the partnership successfully scaled its renewable energy presence by placing a new anaerobic digester facility into service in New York and completing gas upgrading installations in Ohio. The company prioritized balance sheet strength through debt reduction and a strategic refinancing that extended its maturity profile by nearly nine years. Management indicated that the business is transitioning into a phase of lower capital intensity following the completion of major renewable production projects, which supported a 7.7% increase in the annualized distribution rate to unitholders.

Operator: Good morning, and welcome, everyone, to the Suburban Propane Partners Fourth Quarter and Fiscal Year-End Earnings Conference Call. Today's conference is being recorded. [Operator Instructions] At this time, I would like to turn the conference over to Davin D'Ambrosio, Vice President and Treasurer. Please go ahead.

A. D'Ambrosio: Thank you, Audra. Good morning, everyone. Thank you for joining us this morning for our fiscal 2026 fourth quarter and full year earnings conference call. I'm here with Mike Stivala, our President and Chief Executive Officer; Mike Kuglin, Chief Financial Officer; Alex Centeno, our Senior Vice President of our Propane Operations; and Doug Dagan, our Senior Vice President of our Renewable Energy business. This morning, we will review our fiscal 2026 fourth quarter and full year financial results, along with the current outlook for our business. Once we've concluded our prepared remarks, we will open the session to questions.

Michael A. Stivala: Thanks, Davin, and thank you all for joining us this morning. Let me start by acknowledging that yesterday was Veterans Day, and I want to thank all of our amazing veterans for their sacrifices and protection of our freedoms, including the many Suburban Propane veterans and their families. Fiscal 2026 was another outstanding year for Suburban Propane with solid results in our propane operations, meaningful progress in scaling our renewable natural gas or RNG platform, including favorable regulatory developments that have enhanced and are expected to continue to enhance the earnings profile of that business, as well as continued progress in further strengthening the balance sheet.

In our propane operations, the fiscal 2026 heating season presented some of the most sustained cold temperatures and several winter storms across the eastern half of the United States, resulting in strong heat-related demand, which was partially offset by record warm temperatures in the West and near-record warm temperatures in April. In addition, continued growth in our counter seasonal customer base helped mitigate the impact of warmer weather during the shoulder months. Our operating personnel were well prepared to safely and efficiently respond to the surge in demand when and where it came, as well as effectively managing selling prices and expenses amid volatility in commodity and cost environments.

I'm extremely proud of how our teams performed throughout the year while maintaining their focus on our customer base growth and retention initiatives. In our RNG business, we made solid progress across the platform during fiscal 2026. At our Stanfield, Arizona facility, the capital investments and operational enhancements we have made over the past several years are resulting in more stable and reliable production, increased facility uptime and opportunities to organically increase RNG production from our existing feedstock sources. We also completed our capital investment projects for the new anaerobic digester facility in Upstate New York, which was placed into service in July 2026.

And the installation of gas upgrading equipment at our existing anaerobic digester facility in Columbus, Ohio, which is just awaiting final commissioning of the injection site by the local utility. For fiscal 2026, total RNG injection increased 13% compared to the prior year. In addition, pricing in the California LCFS market continued to rise as the year progressed from the mid-$50 range at the beginning to close -- closer to $90 at the end of fiscal 2026.

And in September 2026, the Department of Energy issued updated guidance on the calculation of carbon intensity for manure-based RNG as well as a pathway for food waste feedstocks, which favorably impact the amount of eligible production tax credits, or PTCs, to be earned at each of our facilities under Section 45Z of the Inflation Reduction Act. As a result, we recognized $12.4 million of benefits from PTCs related to our RNG injections and generated $11.3 million of investment tax credits associated with qualifying expenditures from the construction of our Upstate New York facility.

Approximately $3 million of these tax credits, which related to calendar year 2025, were monetized during fiscal 2026, and we expect to monetize the remainder during fiscal 2027. With these projects substantially complete, we are well positioned to continue growing our RNG platform through higher production, capacity optimization and increased feedstock intake as well as a favorable regulatory framework. As a result of the solid performance in both segments of the business, adjusted EBITDA for fiscal 2026 increased $6 million, or 2.2% compared to the prior year. In addition to the improved earnings, we continue to advance our long-term strategic growth initiatives and had a number of notable achievements to highlight.

We acquired and integrated 2 well-run propane businesses in strategic markets in California for a total consideration of $24 million. And subsequent to the end of fiscal 2026, we acquired another high-quality business in Florida for total consideration of approximately $6 million. We continue to identify and foster new market expansions throughout our footprint to establish or extend our presence and grow market share. We have also expanded our business development team to drive growth in specific counter-seasonal customer verticals, such as auto gas, agriculture, power generation and materials handling. The team has done an excellent job identifying new business opportunities and driving growth in these market segments.

We expanded the sales of renewable propane in the transportation and forklift markets to meet customer demand for an even lower carbon intensity alternative to traditional propane with 2.7 million gallons sold in fiscal 2026. We made significant progress in the second year of our multiyear partnership with NASCAR and Speedway Motorsports, where Suburban Propane is the official propane of NASCAR and Speedway Motorsports. By expanding B2B connections for new business development opportunities, increasing brand awareness with fan engagement and a partnership with Ross Chastain, one of the most popular NASCAR drivers on the circuit and eighth-generation watermelon farmer, to serve as a brand ambassador to expand our agricultural base.

Our 2 new RNG facilities, once they reach full run rate capacity, are expected to add approximately 300,000 MMBtus of annual RNG production, reflecting the capital investments we have made in building out our platform. We made good progress advancing our multiyear technology modernization initiative that will simplify the way we operate, consolidate our systems platform and improve the tools we use to serve our customers, delivering a better experience for both our employees and our customers while maintaining our personalized local service model.

And focusing on our balance sheet, we successfully completed the refinancing of our $350 million of 5 7/8% senior notes due in March 2027, with the issuance of $350 million of 6.5% senior notes due in December 2035, effectively extending the maturity of this tranche of debt by nearly 9 years. We raised $23.5 million in net proceeds from the sale of 1.3 million common units under our ATM equity program to support our strategic growth initiatives, and we reduced total debt by more than $25 million, which combined with the increased earnings, improved our net debt to adjusted EBITDA metric to 4.13x at the end of fiscal 2026.

With the capital spending on growth projects in our RNG business substantially behind us, continued progress in strengthening our financial position and our confidence in the long-term outlook for the business, we were pleased to increase our annualized distribution rate to $1.40 per common unit, representing a 7.7% increase over the previous annualized rate. So fiscal 2026 was another very successful year for Suburban Propane, both in terms of our financial performance, and from executing on our long-term strategic growth plans. At the same time, we continue to be -- remain patient and disciplined to maintain financial flexibility through a strong balance sheet.

We are very well positioned for long-term sustainability in the evolving energy landscape and to leverage our strengths to drive growth in both segments of our business. A little later, I'll provide some closing remarks. However, at this time, I'll turn the call over to Mike Kuglin, who will discuss our fourth quarter and full year results in more detail. Mike?

Mike Kuglin: Thanks, Mike, and good morning, everyone. Before discussing our financial results, we give you an overview of changes to our segment reporting that became effective at the end of fiscal 2026. As we've discussed on prior calls, our long-term strategy is focused on creating value for our unitholders by fostering the continued growth of our propane business and development of a scalable renewable energy platform. Our new segment reporting structure is designed to provide investors and analysts with greater visibility to the performance of these 2 businesses and better align our external reporting with how we manage the business and allocate capital. As a result, we now have 2 reportable segments, propane and renewable natural gas.

The propane segment includes activities associated with our propane and renewable propane operations as well as other fuels and related services, while the renewable natural gas segment includes activities associated with production and injection of RNG. This change does not affect our consolidated financial position, results of operations or cash flows. All prior period segment information presented in our fiscal 2026 earnings release has been recast to conform to the new presentation and our Form 10-K will present segment information on the same basis.

As for our financial results, to be consistent with previous reporting, I have excluded the impact of unrealized noncash mark-to-market adjustments on our commodity hedges, which resulted in an unrealized gain of $4.5 million in fiscal 2026 compared to an unrealized gain of $2.4 million in the prior year, along with certain other noncash items, which were identified in the reconciliation of net income to adjusted EBITDA in the press release. Excluding these items, adjusted net income for fiscal 2026 was $138.1 million, or $2.07 per common unit, compared to $128.4 million, or $1.97 per common unit in the prior year.

Adjusted EBITDA for fiscal 2026 was $284 million, an increase of $6 million, or 2.2% compared to the prior year. Retail propane gallons sold in fiscal 2026 were 402.9 million gallons, an increase of 1% compared to the prior year. The increase was driven by colder temperatures during the heating season across much of the eastern half of the United States, which resulted in higher heat-related demand, plus continued growth in our counter-seasonal national account customer base and incremental volumes from recent propane acquisitions.

These benefits were partially offset by considerably warmer temperatures in the western half of the country during the heating season, near record warm temperatures across our operating footprint during the spring and the absence of the incremental volumes we experienced in the prior year in the aftermath of Hurricanes Helene and Milton in the Southeast. With respect to the weather, average temperatures for fiscal 2026 were 8% warmer than normal and 1% cooler than the prior year. In the eastern half of the U.S., average temperatures were 4% warmer than normal and 6% cooler than the prior year, whereas average temperatures in the West were 23% warmer than normal and 14% warmer than the prior year.

From a commodity perspective, average wholesale propane prices for fiscal 2026 were $0.73 per gallon based on Mont Belvieu, which was 7% lower than the prior year. According to the most recent report from the Energy Information Administration, U.S. propane inventories at the end of last week were 104 million barrels, 2% below the prior year, but 4% above historical averages for this time of the year, with much of the surplus concentrated on the Gulf Coast. Despite the continued strength in overall inventories, wholesale propane prices since the end of fiscal 2026 have been volatile and moved sharply higher, driven by strong export demand and global supply constraints resulting from geopolitical events in the Middle East.

Over the past 8 weeks, prices moved from the $0.70 per gallon range in September to more than $1.30 per gallon in early November before retreating to approximately $1.05 per gallon this week. By comparison, propane prices at this time last year were in the mid-$0.60 per gallon range, an increase of roughly 70%. While wholesale prices are higher heading into the fiscal 2027 heating season compared to the past couple of years, our operating personnel have a long track record of effectively managing selling prices through various commodity cycles.

Excluding the impact of the mark-to-market adjustments on our commodity hedges that I mentioned earlier, total gross margin for fiscal 2026 was $888.9 million, an increase of $32.7 million or 3.8% compared to the prior year. The increase was primarily driven by propane unit margin expansion of $0.05 per gallon, or 2.5%, and higher propane volumes sold. Gross margin also benefited from a 13% increase in RNG injection and $12.4 million of PTCs recognized during fiscal 2026 that were recorded as a reduction to cost of products sold on the income statement.

As Mike mentioned, the PTC benefit was calculated using the updated 45Z clean fuels GREET model published by the Department of Energy in September 2026 and is net of estimated monetization discounts and fees. The updated model provides a specific pathway for dairy manure RNG and gets significantly greater credit for the methane emissions avoided by capturing the manure and converting it to RNG, rather than allowing that methane to be released under the farm's prior manure management practices. That change significantly improved the carbon intensity score for RNG compared to the prior model. Based on the updated model, credit represent a value of approximately $46 per MMBtu for D3 RNG before estimated sales discounts.

With respect to expenses, combined operating and G&A expenses increased $22.8 million, or 3.9%, compared to the prior year. The increase was primarily due to higher payroll and benefit-related expenses, increased overtime and other variable operating costs associated with supporting higher customer demand during the heating season, higher fuel and vehicle maintenance costs and higher accruals for self-insurance claims. These increases were partially offset by an insurance recovery of $4.9 million related to the partial settlement of certain claims associated with our acquisition of the RNG production assets in December 2022.

Net interest expense of $76.2 million in fiscal 2026 was flat compared to the prior year as lower borrowings under our revolving credit facility were offset by the higher interest rate for the tranche of senior notes that we refinanced in the first quarter of fiscal 2026. Total capital spending for fiscal 2026 was $85.7 million and included $59.5 million of growth capital. Capital spending increased $13.7 million compared to the prior year primarily due to construction activities at our Columbus, Ohio and Upstate New York RNG facilities, which totaled approximately $32 million in fiscal 2026.

With the major growth projects at Columbus and Upstate New York substantially complete, we expect capital spending to decline in fiscal 2027 to between $60 million and $70 million, which includes $50 million to $55 million for propane operations and $10 million to $15 million for our RNG operations. We expect fiscal 2027 capital spending to be split approximately equally between maintenance and growth initiatives. Turning to our results for the fourth quarter. Consistent with the seasonality of our business, we typically report a net loss for the fourth quarter.

With that said, excluding the effect of certain noncash items in both years, we reported an adjusted net loss of $30.1 million for the fourth quarter, or $0.45 per common unit, compared to a loss of $35.7 million, or $0.54 per common unit in the prior year. Adjusted EBITDA for the fourth quarter was $7.3 million, an increase of $6.6 million compared to the prior year. Retail propane gallons sold were essentially flat compared to the prior year, and RNG injection increased nearly 37% compared to the prior year. Total gross margin increased $15.3 million, or 11.5% compared to the prior year, primarily due to higher propane unit margins, higher RNG injection volumes and a $7.8 million benefit from PTCs.

The PTC benefit recognized during the fourth quarter included a $4.4 million true-up related to credits earned on RNG injections during the first half of calendar 2026, reflecting the impact of the updated 45Z clean fuel model released in September. Combined operating and G&A expenses increased $7.7 million, or 5.8%, primarily due to higher fuel and maintenance costs, higher accruals for self-insurance claims, and professional services fees related to our multiyear initiative to modernize our information technology platform. These increases were partially offset by an insurance recovery of $2 million related to the partial settlement of certain claims associated with our acquisition of the RNG production assets in December 2022.

Excluded from adjusted EBITDA for both the full year and fourth quarter of fiscal 2026 was a $23.1 million impairment charge to fully write down the remaining carrying value of our investment in an early-stage energy technology company, which was reported within other net on the statement of operations. Also excluded was a $7.9 million goodwill impairment charge, representing the full carrying value of goodwill associated with our natural gas and electricity marketing business that has been negatively impacted over the past several years by regulatory actions in New York State that have significantly limited the ability of ESCOs to operate. Turning to our balance sheet.

During the fiscal year, we utilized a combination of cash flows from operating activities and net proceeds of $23.5 million from the issuance of common units under the ATM program to fund our strategic growth initiatives, including the acquisition of 2 propane businesses for a total consideration of $24 million, growth capital expenditures of $34.6 million to advance construction activities and operational enhancements at our RNG production facilities and repayment of outstanding borrowings under our revolving credit facility of $25.4 million. With the improvement in earnings and debt reduction, our consolidated leverage ratio for fiscal 2026 improved to 4.13x compared to 4.29x at the end of fiscal 2025.

With a significant portion of capital spending on the RNG platform largely complete, we expect to generate increasing financial flexibility. We will remain focused on utilizing excess cash flows to further strengthen the balance sheet as opportunities arise to fund strategic growth. With that, I'll turn it back to Mike.

Michael A. Stivala: Thanks, Mike. As I mentioned in my opening remarks, on October 22, we announced that our Board of Supervisors declared an increase in our quarterly distribution to $0.35 per common unit in respect of the fourth quarter of fiscal 2026. That equates to an annualized rate of $1.40 per common unit, 7.7% higher than the previous annualized rate. The quarterly distribution was paid on Tuesday, November 10, to our unitholders of record as of November 3. Our distribution coverage continues to remain healthy at 2.13x for the trailing 12 months ended September 2026 on a maintenance CapEx basis. So just a few closing remarks regarding our long-term strategy.

Over the course of the past 3 fiscal years, we have taken a balanced approach to allocating capital in the execution of our long-term strategic growth plan, which is to foster the growth of our core propane business, while making strategic investments in lower carbon renewable energy alternatives through our renewable -- suburban renewable energy subsidiary. During the 3-year period, we generated excess free cash flow of nearly $390 million and raised approximately $50 million under our ATM equity program. We invested $90 million on propane acquisitions in strategic markets. We invested $85 million to increase the scale of our RNG platform.

We reduced debt by nearly $10 million, and we returned $253.5 million to our unitholders in the form of our quarterly distributions. We will soon enter our 99th year serving local communities across this great nation. Through our long-term strategic growth plan, we are leveraging our core competencies in safety, customer service and logistics, especially in the localized energy distribution markets. The investments that we have made have been very measured and focused on long-term growth and sustainability. We have made great progress in diversifying the business and setting Suburban Propane up to support the energy needs of local communities as the energy system continues to evolve.

The energy evolution is a long journey, one that requires a pragmatic and balanced approach to identifying and fostering energy solutions that can lower greenhouse gas emissions and the overall carbon footprint. However, it also requires solutions that can deliver energy that is reliable, affordable and sustainable. As the interconnected global energy system has shown us, society is faced with the realities that geopolitical events, natural disasters and the surging energy demand from AI and data centers are all factors contributing to not only higher cost to the consumer, but reliability issues with an energy system that cannot evolve overnight.

We are seeing increased recognition that propane can provide the flexibility, redundancy and responsiveness that strengthens resilience while also being relied upon as a prime energy source as infrastructure development catches up with demand. A resilient energy future will require solutions that can be deployed now while supporting the transition ahead, and propane will be relied upon to provide resilient and clean energy for decades to come. With that, I want to thank our more than 3,200 employees for helping make fiscal 2026 another outstanding year for Suburban Propane and for their unwavering commitment to safety for our customers, our people and the communities we serve.

And as always, I hope you and your families remain safe and healthy, and I wish everyone a very happy holiday season, and we appreciate your support and attention and would now like to open the call up to questions. And Audra, could you help us with that?

Operator: [Operator Instructions] We'll take our first question from Gabe Moreen at Mizuho.

Gabriel Moreen: I wanted to ask Mike about just the distribution bump here and your view on kind of the potential cadence going forward. Certainly nice to have a bump here after a couple of years. You mentioned capital allocation and the like. So I'm just wondering kind of how you're viewing that distribution increase going forward as a one-off or something you're targeting more sustainably?

Michael A. Stivala: Well, I think a couple of things there, Gabe. We obviously have done a lot of capital deployment over the last several years, and it's great to get a lot of that behind us now. And we have an RNG platform that is -- has scaled quite a bit. And frankly, the work that we've done to, what I continue to say is suburbanize those assets, which to us, means making them best-in-class assets that we're proud of. And we're -- we're going to continue to run those assets for the -- for the foreseeable future without likely adding to that platform. So when you look at our distribution coverage, we still have very, very healthy coverage.

I'm not going to get ahead of our Board. It's something we talk about every quarter. And I think what our unitholders can take comfort in is that we've set the business up for the long term. We've successfully diversified and we have significant strength in distribution coverage that provides some safety and security of the distribution as it stands with the potential for upside.

Gabriel Moreen: Got it. Appreciate those comments. Congrats, I think, on some of the positive developments in RNG. Maybe can you also -- you spoke to the LCFS credits kind of having a nice bit of an uptick here. Is there any way you can hedge those going forward? Is that something you all have considered?

Michael A. Stivala: I mean, we look at it. But frankly, it's not a very well-developed market at this point. I think what you've seen in those markets is the regulatory developments in California. California sort of recognized that the program wasn't achieving the goals that it was set out to do. And so they adjusted the curve for carbon intensity reduction. And it took a little bit of time for that to work through -- through the excessive credit bank that was built up over the years.

And now that we've actually churned through some of that excess credit, it's good to see that LCFS values have sort of recovered to where they were right around the time when we made our first big investment in the RNG platform in 2022. So I think given where the regulatory environment is right now, I think our outlook is actually that we're pretty -- we have expectations that were pretty stable at these levels for the foreseeable future.

Gabriel Moreen: Got it. And then last one, I can't let you go without a question on propane M&A. It seems like you've done a deal or 2. Just wondering what the landscape looks like at the moment, if any, special factors that are making things more active or less active than usual?

Michael A. Stivala: We're heading into the heating season now. So a lot of the smaller acquisition activity is -- is probably going to wait until after the heating season. So we're constantly evaluating good markets for adding to the platform, and we have a good pipeline of good quality businesses that we have our eye on. But at this stage, I think the businesses will probably run through the heating season, and we'll see what happens with this year's heating season. And usually a lot more of that acquisition activity happens in the spring/summer time frame.

So right now, we're prepared to go into the heating season very strong, and we're looking forward to serving our customers with the best quality service that our field operations is accustomed to delivering.

Technically a Sister Novitiate but still.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Warhammer 40,000: Darktide has announced its newest class, the Sister Novitiate, is arriving on December 7, finally bringing the much anticipated Adepta Sororitas faction to Atoma. Alongside the Skitarii, which was the last class added, the Sisters of Battle have been long–requested by players.

Also similar to the Skitarii, this class has a slightly different design to the other rejects. The Sisters Novitiate can choose from six Acts of Faith instead of blitz and combat abilities, drawing on a new "Faith" resource to use an offensive Neural Whip, or more support based abilities like the Icon of Purity and protective prayers.

Essentially, sisters build their faith through combat via Doctrines and Pledges within the talent tree, which change how it's accrued, allowing them to then unleash up to two chosen Acts of Faith. It sounds fairly similar to the Skitarii's somewhat looser skill and ability tree.

In terms of weapons, we can look forward to:

There's also a meltagun (the first in Darktide), which will be added as a free update alongside for the Veteran class to also use. I'm personally pretty excited for the Condemnor Stake Thrower, a crossbow which pins enemies in place, and as a Zealot main, a new chainsword in the form of the Penitent Eviscerator. There'll likely be a fair bit of crossover between the Zealot and Sister Novitiate.

Overall, this new class is a clever way of including the Adepta Sororitas in Darktide. Lore wise, it would make little sense for a full Sister of Battle to be running around with convicts and rejects, but a sister-in-training, sure, why not?

You can find the new class on Steam where it's set to cost €12 (so I assume £10/$12, the same as the Skitarii was) when it launches on December 7.

HelloNation's Hannah Bouwmeester to Connect with Michigan Manufacturers at MMA Excellence Awards

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CGI Digital and HelloNation will connect with Michigan manufacturers during MMA's annual celebration of manufacturing leadership and innovation.

TRAVERSE CITY, Mich., Nov. 12, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- HelloNation Business Strategist Hannah Bouwmeester will represent CGI Digital and HelloNation at the Michigan Manufacturers Association's 2026 Manufacturing Excellence Awards on Thursday, November 12, at Saint John's Resort in Plymouth, Michigan.

The annual Manufacturing Excellence Awards recognize the individuals, organizations, and products contributing to Michigan's manufacturing sector. The evening includes a reception, dinner, an awards ceremony, and opportunities for manufacturers and industry partners to connect.

CGI Digital and HelloNation partner with the Michigan Manufacturers Association to help members share their expertise, highlight innovation, and tell the stories behind Michigan manufacturing. Through educational articles and videos, manufacturers can address questions customers, prospective employees, and industry partners ask while building greater awareness of their capabilities.

MMA features HelloNation and CGI Digital's digital marketing services as a resource for its members.

For manufacturers, useful educational content can explain how a process works, what buyers should consider when evaluating capabilities, or what a career in the industry can offer. Sharing that knowledge helps people understand the experience, judgment, and innovation behind a company's work.

It also helps make accurate information about a business easier to find as customers, procurement professionals, and job seekers use traditional search engines and AI-powered tools to research their options.

Bouwmeester will be available to discuss how manufacturers can communicate their expertise through HelloNation and explore questions about their presence in AI-driven search. A complimentary AI Audit is available to manufacturers interested in beginning that conversation.

Bitcoin falls to 12-week low as Jensen Huang’s exit from Nvidia rattles markets

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Huang resigned as the company's president, CEO and board member effective immediately, while MSCI plans to remove bitcoin treasury firm Strategy from its indexes on Nov. 30.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell to its lowest level in nearly 12 weeks on Thursday, trading as low as $71,200, as markets reacted to Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's abrupt departure and continued uncertainty surrounding potential selling pressure from MSCI index removals.

Bitcoin was down 3.4% to $71,600 at publication time. Other major crypto assets, including Ethereum, Solana and XRP, also declined more than 3.5% over the past 24 hours.

More than $750 million in crypto positions were liquidated in the past 24 hours, including over $634 million in long positions, according to CoinGlass.

"We remain cautious on BTC as it tests $73k, the level we flagged as first potential support, with no convincing signs of size stepping in at these prices," Jasper De Maere, OTC trader at Wintermute, said Thursday morning. "Some bluechip names are seeing activity, but without large buyers the majors need ETF flows to carry them. That changes if flows turn structurally positive for several days in a row."

Meanwhile, MSCI confirmed it will remove Strategy from its indexes on Nov. 30, raising the prospect of selling in the bitcoin proxy. Wintermute described the move as "a passive-selling risk on a BTC proxy where we have no size estimate."

Nvidia's leadership turmoil had been building over the past week. The company launched an investigation into Huang last weekend following reports that he had been a "target of a foreign intelligence effort" amid an alleged affair with a Chinese spy. On Wednesday, the New York Times reported that Huang was also having an affair with a woman who directly reports to him.

On Thursday morning, Huang resigned as president, CEO, and board member, effective immediately. CFO Colette Kress was appointed interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent successor. Kress joined Nvidia in 2013 and will remain CFO during the transition.

President Donald Trump also weighed in, saying former Apple CEO Tim Cook should take the helm of the AI chip developer. "[T]here is only one man to take his place," Trump wrote on Truth Social. "A Legend replaces a Legend!"

Nvidia shares opened Thursday down 12%, extending their decline this month. Nvidia perpetual futures on Hyperliquid generated $2.34 billion in 24-hour trading volume.
