Howard Lutnick is next Trump official top Dems want on the chopping block amid NVIDIA scandal

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Congressional Democrats tell Fox News Digital that members of the administration, including Lutnick, Rubio, Homan and Miller could be on their chopping block in the 120th Congress

EXCLUSIVE : Top House Democrats are calling for an investigation into Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over allegations involving NVIDIA's former CEO's reported relationship with an alleged Chinese spy.

NVIDIA CEO Jensen Huang resigned this week following allegations that he had an affair with Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed who is suspected of being a Chinese spy.

In the midst of the spy scandal, multiple investigative reports emerged claiming Lutnick received a classified memo warning him about Huang’s relationship with alleged Chinese spy Alice Huang, of no familial relation to the NVIDIA CEO.

"Howard Lutnick needs to be investigated over his role in Jensen Huang and the Chinese spy, we're going to have an investigation into those allegations, that's completely unacceptable," Rep. Ted Lieu, D-Calif., who is expected to become chairman of the House Democratic Caucus, told Fox News Digital.

FOREIGN SPY ‘HONEYTRAPS’ TARGETING AMERICA’S TECH ELITE SPARK ALARM AMID NVIDIA CEO SCANDAL

Howard Lutnick joined the Trump administration as Commerce Secretary in 2025 after co-chairing Trump’s transition team. (Aaron Schwartz/CNP/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

Lieu alleged that despite Lutnick being briefed on the reported relationship between Huang and a woman identified by U.S. intelligence as a Chinese spy, he failed to take action.

Rep. Ro Khanna, D-Calif., a vice chair of the Congressional Progressive Caucus and member of the House Select Committee on China, also expressed support for investigating Lutnick and did not rule out examining Secretary of State Marco Rubio's involvement, as well.

"I think we need transparency," Khanna told Fox News Digital. "I think we need to understand what they knew."

PROGRESSIVE GROUP'S 182-PAGE BLUEPRINT MAPS OUT POTENTIAL DEM INVESTIGATIONS INTO TRUMP, CORPORATE ALLIES

"Rubio knew about the Chinese spy, and then got invited to the China trip," Khanna continued. "We need to get to the bottom of this."

Lutnick and Rubio did not respond to Fox News Digital’s request for comment.

(L-R) US Secretary of the Interior Doug Burgum, US Secretary of State Marco Rubio, US President Donald Trump, US Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth and US Secretary of Commerce Howard Lutnick listen, during a cabinet meeting, in the Cabinet Room of the White House in Washington, DC, of April 30, 2025. (JIM WATSON/AFP via Getty Images)

REBECCA GRANT: JENSEN HUANG OWES AMERICA AN APOLOGY, AND NVIDIA OWES INVESTORS ANSWERS

Many left-leaning lawmakers are jumping on the chance to name the Cabinet official they want to see face scrutiny when Democrats take back control of the House in January.

However, neither Khanna or Lieu shed light on how they would investigate, prosecute or remove Lutnick, and potentially Rubio. Neither of the Cabinet secretaries have been formally accused or indicted of wrongdoing in connection with the NVIDIA case.

House Minority Leader Rep. Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., who is currently running unopposed for the Democratic nomination for Speaker of the House, did not respond to Fox News Digital’s request for comment on whether he spoke to either lawmakers on their desires to take action against Lutnick and Rubio.

HAKEEM JEFFRIES DECLARES BID FOR HOUSE SPEAKER AFTER GOP'S MIDTERM MASSACRE

The lawmakers' remarks signal that congressional Democrats could use their incoming majority to investigate senior Trump administration officials over their handling of alleged Chinese intelligence threats.

Rep. Ro Khanna (D-CA) appears on "Meet the Press" in Washington D.C., Sunday, Sep. 1, 2024 (William B. Plowman/NBC via Getty Images)

It is unclear whether Republican lawmakers will support the Democrats' investigations. But following Huang’s resignation, House Select Committee on China Chairman John Moolenaar, R-Mich., announced that the outgoing CEO has agreed to brief congressional committees following the scandal.

He also advocated for Congress to act quickly to pass bipartisan export control legislation in the year-end national defense bill.

Moolenaar welcomed the resignation and added that companies such as NVIDIA should "never jeopardize our nation’s security by giving Chinese companies access to their most advanced chips," and called on them to "change its corporate practices and appoint leadership that makes national security its top priority."

Il Napoli pensa al mercato di riparazione. Ma quali devono essere gli obiettivi principali per migliorare questa rosa? Ecco cosa hanno detto gli opinionisti della radio di Tuttomercatoweb.com.

Stefano Impallomeni: "Il Napoli ha 15 punti in classifica dopo 11 giornate, una media bassa. Allegri accetta il Napoli perché pensa, al netto di un non mercato, sia comunque forte. Oggi tutti a dire che il mercato va fatto. Io credo che c'è nello sport chi arriva primo e chi arriva indietro. Quindi che mercato deve fare? Anche se gli compri Pelè a questa squadra è difficile che recuperi per fare tanti punti".

Ci sono giocatori che hanno contratti onerosi e non si muoveranno da Napoli se non dopo aver trovato un'adeguata situazione. You're probably aware by now of the scandal at Nvidia. Outgoing CEO Jensen Huang has announced his departure following allegations of two extramarital affairs within the last week. This is reverberating through the tech and games industries, causing Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney to weigh in.

He did so on Twitter, where he expressed considerable respect for Huang. "A sad day for Nvidia, developers, America, and all humanity," he writes. "Jensen founded and ran one of the few companies to reach a massive scale while driving massive continued innovation and ubiquity, while others fell into the trap of ever-increasing extraction."

A sad day for NVIDIA, developers, America, and all humanity. Jensen founded and ran one of the few companies to reach a massive scale while driving massive continued innovation and ubiquity, while others fell into the trap of ever-increasing extraction.November 12, 2026

His first post referenced a tweet he made in 2024, celebrating Huang's position at the forefront of Nvidia and highlighting how closely Epic and the hardware manufacturer have worked in the past, particularly through the Unreal Engine. How that business agreement continues is something that'll need to be worked out with Huang's successor.

The Saga of Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s Fake Leather Jacket

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The internet loved his outrageous new look. There was just one problem.

There are several ways to respond when you've admitted to an affair, your company's shares are tumbling, and even the White House is discussing your private life. Turning up in a spectacularly studded Saint Laurent leather jacket is one of them. Resigning is another.

For a while, it seemed like Jensen Huang, the 63-year-old former CEO of Nvidia, had opted for the former. A photograph showing the tech billionaire in an archival Hedi Slimane YSL biker jacket went viral this week. The internet was delighted. There was just one problem: The photograph was fake.

The resignation, which came just this morning, was real.

As CNN reported, the image had been doctored from a genuine photograph taken at a Seoul airport in June. Nvidia, which one might imagine has other things to deal with right now, confirmed it wasn't real.

But perhaps the reason so many people believed it was that Huang already dresses like this. Leather jackets are his uniform. He reportedly owns 30 or 40 of them, including a snazzy reptile-effect number worn to deliver his company's keynote at the Consumer Electronics Show.

And Hedi Slimane was the perfect designer to bring into the fantasy. The Frenchman, who ran Saint Laurent from 2012 to 2016, made his name dressing skinny rock stars and turning their disheveled, devil-may-care attitude—later rebranded as so-called “indie sleaze”—into expensive luxury fashion. His leather jackets were practically a uniform for men who were determined to look as though they didn't care what anyone thought and/or having a midlife crisis.

For a billionaire in the middle of a spectacularly public scandal, it was quite a look. One Instagram commenter described Huang as the first person to achieve "negative aura" in archival Hedi.

There's another irony. Huang has previously said his wife Lori—i.e., the wronged party in all this—helps choose his clothes.

And then there's the technology itself. Nvidia makes the chips that power the AI revolution, including the tools capable of ... producing convincing fake photographs.

In conclusion then, the image wasn't real. But as a portrait of a billionaire in the middle of a spectacularly public crisis, we so wanted it to be.

Hugging Face CEO vows to keep fighting for open-source AI after Jensen Huang’s exit

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Hugging Face CEO Clément Delangue briefly commented on Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang’s departure on Thursday, calling it a “really sad day.” Nvidia had announced its plan to acquire the platform for open-source AI models in September for $12.9 billion, making it the second-largest acquisition in the company’s history, just behind that of chipmaker Groq’s assets for $20 billion in December 2025.

“This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day! But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!,” Delangue wrote on X.

This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day!But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!

The post is interesting as it implies that Huang’s departure is potentially worrying to open-source advocates, and he’s attempting to offer reassurance.

As Nvidia’s CEO, Huang was known as a strong advocate for open-source AI and open-weight models, which is why there could be concerns about how a company that’s no longer under his leadership will now approach this space.

After Nvidia announced its plans to acquire Hugging Face, the U.S. Department of Justice began making inquiries about how the deal would impact competition in the AI market, Reuters reported in October. While Nvidia pledged that the platform would preserve open support, it would also allow the company to control a major distribution channel for AI models.

The Hugging Face deal is still scheduled to close in the first half of 2027. Requests for further comment on the deal status and whether Hugging Face has spoken yet with Nvidia’s interim CEO Collette Kress were not returned.

Huang’s advocacy for open-source and open-weight AI has long extended beyond Hugging Face. In July, the CEO published an AI policy letter as his first-ever post on the social network X. The letter, which was signed by 25 companies, including Microsoft, Meta, Hugging Face, IBM, Palantir, and others, urged the U.S. government not to over-restrict open-weight AI models. It said models that “anyone can download, inspect, modify, and run on their own infrastructure” are an important part of the AI ecosystem because they make AI “more accessible, adaptable, and widely available.”

Huang also wrote that open-weight models expand access to the AI economy, strengthen competition, and give customers greater control, as they know they won’t become locked into a single provider. “Open models strengthen safety and cybersecurity, accelerate innovation and diffusion, and enable sovereignty,” his post on X stated.

For my first post, I’m sharing a letter @NVIDIA signed on why open models matter.AI will transform every industry, power every company, and be built by every country.Open models strengthen safety and cybersecurity, accelerate innovation and diffusion, and enable sovereignty.… pic.twitter.com/t02bi51N4C

In August, Nvidia also announced a $6 billion deal with the coding startup Poolside to help build out an open AI ecosystem in the U.S. to help the country compete against Chinese companies like DeepSeek and Kimi K3, The Wall Street Journal reported at the time. Last month, Nvidia expanded its Nemotron family of open-weight models with a large 120-billion-parameter model for agents used by the enterprise as well as with two new lightweight Bolt models that run on PCs.

Earlier this year, Huang promoted open-source AI during his January keynote address at the Consumer Electronics Show in Las Vegas, noting that open-source models have “really revolutionized artificial intelligence.

Welch’s holiday microdrama embraces Y2K nostalgia as Gen Z become parents

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The five-episode series features the actress who voiced Kim in “Kim Possible” and a member of The Cheetah Girls group.

Welch’s is jumping on the microdrama trend as consumers head into the holiday season. “Before the Toast” features many of the trappings typically associated with the format — like absurdly high stakes and plenty of drama — and a nostalgia-fueled cast to promote the brand’s line of sparkling juices during a time of year when demand is usually high.

Joining Romano in the series is her husband, Brendan Rooney, Sabrina Bryan of The Cheetah Girls and Heather Morris, from “Glee.” Storylines largely revolve around navigating holiday season chaos with young children and creating new memories.

The actors chosen for the campaign, in addition to its strategic placement around family-friend holiday programming, may help the brand reach families with younger children. The demand for sparkling juices has gone up as consumers look for alternatives to soda and alcoholic beverages. This season also sees the introduction of Welch’s Sparkling Low Sugar Grape, which is available at Walmart.

The cast may also be nostalgic for Gen Z parents who grew up on Disney Channel shows, many of whom are sharing the media with their own children.

As part of the campaign, the brand teamed up with Instacart to give away full-size bottles of its products in the lead-up to Thanksgiving and Christmas. Shoppers are able to claim the offer via the product card on the Instacart app.

Microdramas are being embraced by brands looking to reach consumers. The format has proven popular with Gen Z, who have grown used to watching short, vertical videos on their phones. The scale of efforts have varied, from just a few episodes to P&G’s 50-part series for Native. Welch’s isn’t the first beverage brand to jump on the microdrama bandwagon. In May, Dr Pepper promoted the return of its Creamy Coconut product with a four-episode series in which a young woman has a fleeting love affair with a can of the dirty soda-inspired flavor.

Welch’s, which operates as an agricultural cooperative and does not release earnings statements, replaced its CMO position with a chief brand and innovation officer in October of last year. Andrew Hartshorn, who previously held roles at PepsiCo and Danone, assumed the role. Shortly after he arrived, the brand introduced a new kid mascot, marking the first time in 25 years that Welch’s creative starred a child character.

Welp, that's it, the face of Nvidia is no longer the face of Nvidia. Jensen Huang served as CEO of the company since it was founded over three decades ago, and now he's out, replaced by the new interim CEO Colette Kress, who was previously Chief Financial Officer (exactly what we predicted, by the way).

This is in the wake of two affair scandals surrounding the former CEO. The first affair Huang admitted to, and it's alleged that the other party in the tango is a lady who is currently accused of being a Chinese spy. The second is a reported affair that Huang allegedly had with Nvidia's VP of healthcare, Kimberly Powell.

Many people expected Huang's resignation in the lead-up to this, so it is no big surprise that it has now been announced by Nvidia. But now that it's official, the response has largely been as expected: a combination of disappointment at his resignation and praise for his time as Nvidia CEO.

President Trump certainly expressed as much, saying Huang has done a "fantastic job." Trump also has a suggestion for who should take over: ex-Apple CEO Tim Cook: "Now that Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, who has done a fantastic job, has resigned, there is only one man to take his place - Tim Cook of Apple. A Legend replaces a Legend! President Donald J. Trump."

Hugging Face co-founder Clement Delangue expresses the general sentiment: "This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day!"

This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day! But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!November 12, 2026

Nvidia recently set out to buy Hugging Face, the open-source AI platform, and given the latter agreed, it makes sense for the company to assuage any fears over the news of Huang's resignation. Julien Chaumond, Hugging Face co-founder and CTO, has expressed that "the mission has not and will not change."

Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney has expressed his sadness, too: "A sad day for Nvidia, developers, America, and all humanity. Jensen founded and ran one of the few companies to reach a massive scale while driving massive continued innovation and ubiquity, while others fell into the trap of ever-increasing extraction."

Sweeney's response isn't a surprise. Before Huang's resignation was announced, both Tim Sweeney and Elon Musk certainly expressed high praise for Huang. The former said, "This would be an Apple firing Steve Jobs level of idiocy from Nvidia’s board if they did that, and a grievous harm to the United States of America." And the latter said it would be "an honor" to hire him.

It's also no surprise, then, that the Epic CEO has banged the same drum a few additional times since the announcement, calling it "far too much government heavy handedness" and taking a dig at Nvidia's new interim CEO for waiting a week until discussing everything in a company meeting.

Even ancient file-sharing platform LimeWire has jumped in on the news.

jensen huang come work at limewire we don't care who you date just bring the leather jacketNovember 12, 2026

Over the past couple of days before the resignation, there were differing opinions on whether Huang should resign or not. Some thought so, but the more general sentiment across social media seemed to be that regardless of any alleged affairs, he is a good CEO and it will be very damaging for Nvidia to lose him.

The reactions now, in the wake of Huang's resignation, seem to concur.

That is, apart from the US Select Committee on China, which is decidedly more stern:

"Jensen Huang’s firing from the company he founded is an unfortunate end that could have been avoided. It is in our national interest for NVIDIA and our other AI champions to succeed, but American companies should never jeopardize our nation’s security by giving Chinese companies access to their most advanced chips. Nvidia must now change its corporate practices and appoint leadership that makes national security its top priority. In Congress we must act quickly to pass bipartisan export control legislation in the year-end national defense bill."

Chairman @RepMoolenaar released the following statement in the wake of Jensen Huang's resignation from @NVIDIA: https://t.co/sCJIYnE7WoNovember 12, 2026

The political bigwigs in general will be looking to get from this whatever they can. New York senator and Senate Democratic Leader Chuck Schumer says, "Despite Jensen Huang’s resignation, Congress must demand answers from Mr. Huang and others at Nvidia about how they may have put U.S. national security at risk."

Despite Jensen Huang’s resignation, Congress must demand answers from Mr. Huang and others at Nvidia about how they may have put U.S. national security at risk. The Trump administration must also answer for what it did once it learned about Mr. Huang’s relationship with a suspected Chinese intelligence asset. Were government officials told not to investigate because of Trump’s relationship with Mr. Huang? What did Trump know when he personally invited Mr. Huang aboard Air Force One? Americans deserve to know that those at the highest levels of power are putting America first, not their bottom line or their personal friendships.November 12, 2026

As far as we're concerned as PC gamers, much of this might be by-the-by. Whoever heads up Nvidia, the company long since stopped putting gaming anywhere near the centre of its focus. AI is the name of the game, now.

I can't help but feel a little sad, though. Huang has been one of the biggest names in PC gaming hardware for decades, but no longer. Whatever you think of the man himself and whether he should have resigned, there is indeed something sad about his era being over.

Trump opens door to immigration deal, but Republicans have one condition

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"Obama did this very thing. They still call him the 'deporter in chief'. And he did oversee a lot of deportations. But those numbers were also cooked. The books were cooked because they were counting things as deportations that were, in fact, just turnaways at the border and many other things baked into those Obama numbers. But then what he did when he tried to push his Dreamers and all his things, and he said, look, I've deported all these people. I've done what you wanted with that. Now we need to start looking at amnesty and legal status. But this is the exact same thing that we're seeing today.

Some Senate Republicans are open to revisiting immigration policy after President Donald Trump floated a possible deal with Democrats, but they insist amnesty remains off the table.

Republicans, after sweeping midterm losses, are willing to open up the immigration policy debate after voters made clear that they didn't support the administration’s approach: a Fox News poll published in September found that 53% of voters believed that Trump's immigration policy had gone too far.

But there’s one thing that Senate Republicans won’t agree to: amnesty.

WHITE HOUSE DOUBLES DOWN AFTER TRUMP ANGERS BASE BY FLOATING ILLEGAL ALIEN DEAL WITH DEMS

President Donald Trump arrives to deliver remarks during a campaign rally at the Rock Hill Sports and Event Center in Rock Hill, S.C., on Oct. 29, 2026. Trump was in South Carolina rallying support for local Republican candidates ahead of the midterm elections. (Getty Images)

"I trust President Trump," Sen. Jim Banks, R-Ind., told Fox News Digital. "He's gonna do what's right, but as far as I'm concerned, amnesty is off the table. I'll never go for it."

The door creaked open on the idea when Trump, during an interview with "60 Minutes" that aired over the weekend, said that his administration’s mass deportation and enforcement policies might be "too tough."

"Get the killers out, get the rapists out, get all the rough ones out, get the drug dealers out," Trump said. "But let the people that are here — and in some cases here for 10 and 15 years — work a deal for them."

The administration quickly sought to clarify Trump’s comments, with Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin affirming that there would be "no amnesty" shortly after.

HOUSE GOP ERUPTS AT STEPHEN MILLER AS TRUMP ELEVATES HIM AFTER MIDTERM MASSACRE: 'MEGALOMANIAC'

Still, Republicans are willing to navigate the complex issue as Democrats gear up to take power next year. Some have been calling for action on the matter for months.

Sen. Thom Tillis, R-N.C., one of the administration’s sharper Republican critics on immigration policy who is retiring at the end of the year, told Fox News Digital that as early as March he texted Trump and suggested it would be the "perfect time" to codify "all the stuff that has effectively sealed the border and used it as an opportunity to get merit-based immigration reform."

"I also said in that text, though, was, in order for this to be successful, Stephen Miller either needs to be out of the room or on his meds," Tillis said. "And so he's the reason they're walking it back is because they don't want to do it, because they have some false assumption that that's gonna hurt our base."

"Our base is fine with merit-based immigration reform. President Trump is fine with merit-based immigration reform," he continued. "And if we don't get the stuff codified that has effectively sealed the border, it's gonna be on them when the border gets opened up if a Democrat wins in ‘28."

The Senate, just before Trump won the 2024 election, was on the cusp of a bipartisan immigration deal when Democrats controlled the chamber. The deal collapsed after Trump criticized it and Senate Republicans withdrew support.

"There's a lot of scar tissue on this issue, but, you know, the president, because he secured the borders, he has credibility on the issue, and he has expressed a willingness," Senate Majority Leader John Thune, R-S.D., said. "I have not seen anything yet that's being proposed, but we will certainly hear them out, and if there's something he wants to do there, have a conversation about it."

JEFFRIES VOWS DEMOCRATS WON’T ‘GIVE A DIME’ TO TRUMP’S DEPORTATION EFFORTS

Sen. James Lankford, R-Okla., accused Senate Democrats of being more afraid of "ICE than they are of Iran." (Allison Robbert/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

The bipartisan deal was the product of negotiations among Sens. James Lankford, R-Okla., Chris Murphy, D-Conn., and former Sen. Kyrsten Sinema, and would have funneled billions for border security and shuttered the border to most asylum seekers, among several other policy changes. But conservatives balked at the deal, and Trump’s influence from the campaign trail ultimately killed it.

Years later, Lankford is again in a prime position to work on a deal with Democrats, given that he is expected to become the ranking Republican on the Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee next year.

"If I’m ranking on Homeland Security, I’m right in the thick of it, whether you like it or not," Lankford said.

Though opinions vary on what exactly the GOP’s approach would be going forward, many Republicans agree that the administration’s enforcement policies, particularly under former Homeland Security Secretary Kristi Noem, should not make a comeback.

"I think you have to make sure, first and foremost, that how you do your enforcement is not like the administration started under Kristi Noem," Sen. Ted Budd, R-N.C., told Fox News Digital.

"I think people are for some enforcement, but not in a cruel way. And there were so many, there was a lack of training, lack of proper methodologies, and I think people want to take enforcement seriously."

"But, again, I would say no amnesty at all," he continued. "We don't want to go there. Ronald Reagan, a great president, one of his few regrets, but significant regrets, was amnesty. And I don't think Trump wants to make that mistake."

Alex Miller is a writer for Fox News Digital covering the U.S. Senate.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Newegg has refreshed its bundle deals and have recently come out with a couple of solid deals that help take some of the sting out of building a PC in these trying times. The latest combo pairs one of the fastest gaming CPUs in the Ryzen 5 9800X3D, 32GB of Corsair DDR5-6000 RAM, and a premium motherboard with MSI’s X870E Carbon Wifi for only $1,250.98, saving $119 compared to buying the parts individually. In fact, buying this RAM kit alone would cost $509.99, so if you apply the discount to the RAM, it’s just $390 and cheaper than any comparable standalone kit, which looks to be around $460 at the time of this writing. Newegg also throws in a free Rosewill 360mm AIO ($90 value) to sweeten the pot.

The RAM portion of the bundle, Corsair’s Vengeance RGB 32GB (2x16GB) DDR5-6000 CL36 RAM kit, is currently available on Newegg for an eye-watering $509.99 when bought separately. The good news is that the discount brings this RAM down to something reasonable for the times. This black kit matches the MSI X870E Carbon board in the combo and features a frosted RGB light bar that runs the length of the sticks. If RGB lighting isn’t your thing and you prefer a stealthy, unassuming look, you can turn it off. The DDR5-6000 speed lands in the sweet spot for the AM5 platform and is a worthwhile, good-looking option. The SK Hynix ICs under the heatsink pair nicely with the MSI board and offer plenty of overclocking headroom.

Save $119 on this 3-item Newegg combo that includes 32GB of Corsair Vengeance DDR5-6000 CL36 RAM, the awesome-for-gaming Ryzen 5 9800X3D CPU, and a premium motherboard with MSI's X870E Carbon Wifi.

Next is one of the most popular and fastest current-generation gaming CPUs: the Ryzen 7 9800X3D. This 8-core/16-thread Zen5-based processor has a 4.6 GHz base clock and a 5.2 GHz max boost, plenty fast for any task. The X3D variant's 96MB of L3 cache helps with gaming (among other things), making it one of the fastest gaming CPUs available. It is also a well-rounded performer that excels in games and suits most applications.

Last but certainly not least is the premium MSI X870E Carbon Wifi motherboard (one we reviewed in the past. We loved the robust power delivery (perfect for the 9800X3D or greater), the dual Ethernet ports, EZ features, storage options (4x SATA, 4x M.2 sockets - one PCie 5.0) and ample connectivity on the rear I/O, including four USB Type-C ports (2x 40 Gbps). The all-black motherboard sports a cool RGB design element of the MSI dragon on the VRM heatsink to break up the monotony and lend some bling to the inside of your chassis. It’s a great motherboard to build your AM5 rig around.

In the end, we like this combo as this, and others like it, help take some of the pain out of buying a PC today. Priced at $1,250.98 with a savings of $118.99 and a free 360mm Rosewill Frostmax Digital ARGB AIO ($90 value), it’s one of the better bundles available on Newegg right now. Get it while supplies last.

Nvidia's share price has seemingly grown by around 10% since pre-market trading lows.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

This week has been a mess for Nvidia, with its CEO, Jensen Huang, admitting to an affair on Sunday, only for a second affair allegation to come out yesterday. Today, Nvidia shares started as low as $151 in pre-market trading, but have since bounced back to between $163 and $166 in the opening hours of Nasdaq trading after the announcement of Jensen Huang's retirement as CEO.

One might assume a downward trajectory with Huang's resignation, and CFO Colette Kress replacing him, as it could flag instability to shareholders, but the stock price now sits where it once sat on Wednesday, November 11 at 5 PM ET. The Nvidia stock price has been unruly this week, dipping by $15 between Friday night and Monday morning, then climbing to $181 by the end of Monday, then once again dropping to $167 on Tuesday.

But, importantly, this morning saw the worst trade open for Nvidia stock since June 2025, just before the memory crisis started. The jump from $151 to around $165 represents a 10% increase for the company.

Naturally, stocks are a fickle business, and, famously, the 'dead cat bounce' effect describes a moment in a stock decline where it briefly recovers. But Nvidia, even with a big loss in market cap value, is still an incredibly successful company worth multiple trillions of dollars at the time of writing.

I had my eye on the trading value this morning, somewhat expecting a decline after the opening, given the drop overnight, and it's easy to see why. Jensen Huang is a cofounder of Nvidia, which at one point was the most valuable company in the world. He has been the president and CEO of the company since 1993, and he's no doubt been instrumental in the business and its success.

This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day!But I want to make one thing clear: we’ll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever. This is too important to slow down now. Let’s go!November 12, 2026

And this morning, many big names in the tech industry expressed their sadness at his departure. Tim Sweeney, the Epic Games founder and CEO, said it was "A sad day for Nvidia, developers, America, and all humanity", and Clement Delangue, the co-founder and CEO of AI platform Hugging Face, said, "This whole thing sucks, and I wish none of it had happened. It’s a really sad day". Jim Cramer, the host of Mad Money on CNBC, said "Jensen we hardly knew yah. Just plain awful...."

Colette Kress has taken over, but only in the interim, and Nvidia is no doubt shopping around for the person who will replace Huang. Bets are open on who exactly that person could be, from the CEO of Intel, AMD, and Arm, all the way to Elon Musk. And Donald Trump even wants ex-Apple CEO Tim Cook to take the chair. It's anyone's guess, really, and a good sign that it's also hard to guess exactly what will happen when it comes to stock prices.

"We believe that such a historic occasion deserves our full attention"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

We're now one week away from the launch of GTA 6, and Palworld studio Pocketpair has announced that it'll be giving devs excited to play Rockstar's game the time off to do so, because "we believe that such a historic occasion deserves our full attention."

I don't know about you, but I suspect that November 19 may see a lot of people around the world calling in sick to their jobs thanks to a sudden bout of illness that's going to take them until at least after the weekend to recover from. Pocketpair might be anticipating that itself, as a staff notice also shared on Twitter confirms that they'll all be able to "undertake a business trip to Vice City," if they please.

"On November 19, millions of gamers around the world will find themselves sitting at school or work, gazing longingly out the window, knowing that a new era of gaming is about to begin," the notice starts. "At Pocketpair, we believe that such a historic occasion deserves our full attention.

"Therefore," it continues, "after careful consideration, we have decided that all employees who intend to play Grand Theft Auto VI will be granted a special five-day holiday." That period, of course, runs from November 19 to November 23, during which "employees will be excused from their regular duties and are instead encouraged to undertake a business trip to Vice City. We hope everyone enjoys their time away from the office and returns safely from their travels."

All Pocketpair employees have been granted special leave to attend to an urgent five-day business trip in Vice City when GTA6 launches. Please see the attached notice for details. https://t.co/qJjJ7IvhhYNovember 12, 2026

I genuinely thought that this was a joke post at first, but separate tweets from Pocketpair CEO Takuro Mizobe and head of publishing John "Bucky" Buckley indicate otherwise. Mizobe says (via machine translation) that he'll be taking time off to play GTA 6 himself, while Bucky admits that "things might be a bit quiet" while the team is busy playing Rockstar's new title.

Bucky previously joked that Slay the Spire 2's release had delayed Palworld's 1.0 launch by "at least a whole day" after Mizobe got a telling off for staying up all night to play it, and I'd imagine the company might want to avoid a similar situation here. Better than having everyone in the office getting no work done, right?

Pocketpair wants to make it clear that the GTA 6 holiday won't affect any of its schedules, though: "Please note that update work for Palworld and Craftopia is proceeding smoothly and is expected to be carried out as scheduled," it adds. Let the devs have their fun, basically.

Bari, sciolto il Comitato dei Garanti: "Impossibile portare avanti il nostro lavoro"

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Protagonista negli ultimi giorni di un botta e risposta con la proprietà del Bari, il Comitato dei Garanti è stato ufficialmente sciolto nella giornata di oggi a distanza di cinque mesi dalla sua nascita che, nei piani del sindaco Vito Leccese, doveva accompagnare il percorso per il superamento della multiproprietà, la cessione del club e garantire alla città il massimo livello di informazione, trasparenza e vigilanza sul rispetto degli impegni assunti dalla proprietà del club.

Come riporta Telebari infatti lo scioglimento del Comitato era nell’aria da qualche giorno, proprio a seguito delle parole del portavoce Sassanelli che aveva lamentato la mancata e incompleta trasmissione della documentazione chiesta alla proprietà del Bari. Oggi i membri del comitato si sono incontrati con il sindaco e al termine dell’incontro è stato annunciato lo scioglimento del gruppo.

A parlare dopo l’incontro è stato l’ex presidente della Corte d’Appello di Bari Francesco Cassano, uno dei membri del gruppo: “L'esito di quest'incontro era inevitabile. Credo che il comitato sia stato messo nell'impossibilità di operare utilmente. Utilmente significava, alla luce degli accordi tra la società e l'amministrazione comunale, capire se si intendesse effettivamente avviare un percorso che portasse alla vendita della società massimo entro il 2027. Non siamo in grado di poter accertare questo scopo. Conseguentemente non c'è una ragione per cui dobbiamo proseguire nel nostro impegno. - ha proseguito Cassano come riporta Tuttobari.com - Siamo grati al sindaco perché ci ha chiamati. Abbiamo provato a dare una mano per risolvere un problema molto delicato. Abbiamo fatto tutto il possibile per tentare di avviare un dialogo vero con la società. Riteniamo che i punti di vista siano legittimamente diversi. Abbiamo inoltre riscontrato la volontà di non collaborare pienamente secondo quelle che erano le nostre necessità. Abbiamo tentato già a luglio di avviare un dialogo con la società e non abbiamo avuto risposta. Poi finite le vicissitudini giudiziarie che hanno coinvolto la società, pensavamo potesse partire un dialogo proficuo.

Il 17 ottobre c'è stato un importante incontro, in cui abbiamo richiesto la documentazione iniziale ai rappresentanti della società. Ebbene anche quella non ci è stata fornita per intero e poi sono emerse delle inadempienze di fondo. La società ritiene che noi non abbiamo dei poteri e che sia essa stessa a decidere ciò che ci è sufficiente per svolgere il nostro compito".

Hang On, Firefight Is Coming To Halo: Campaign Evolved

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Halo: Campaign Evolved isn't dead, people!

Firefight has just been announced as a free update for Halo: Campaign Evolved, arriving sometime this winter. The series' staple co-op PvE mode is locked in, with more details set to be shared at Halo Fest on December 12-13.

Halo: Campaign Evolved received some backlash for its lack of multiplayer options, particularly PvP and Firefight. I thought the criticism was understandable, but perhaps a little unfair given the game was always marketed as a campaign remake.

"For nearly two decades, Firefight has been a Halo favorite, challenging Spartans to team up, hold the line, and survive wave after wave of enemies. The tradition continues this winter as we add the four-player cooperative PvE mode to Halo: Campaign Evolved in a free update.

We’re kicking things off tonight at XBOX FanFest, where attendees will be the first to jump in and experience Firefight across new maps and armed with plenty of weapons to help even the odds."

Forgive my opening sentiment, but I honestly wasn't expecting anything new to come from Halo Studios. Especially after the developer was essentially reduced to a skeleton team, with future Halo production being handed over to Activision.

I'm a huge fan of Halo: Campaign Evolved, and whilst I'm still sad the teased Halo 2 remake seems unlikely, you should have seen me leap out of my seat when I spotted the announcement that Firefight was on its way. Hoorah!

Roughly two-thirds of its sales were physical

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is selling more physical copies and digital copies in Japan, and by some margin to boot.

Japanese magazine Famitsu's latest sales chart numbers are in, with The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time unsurprisingly at the top. According to its data, The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake for Nintendo Switch 2 sold 537,041 physical copies at Japanese retailers.

Earlier this week, Nintendo confirmed that the latest Zelda release had sold over 5.2 million copies within four days of launch, making it "the highest global sales for any Nintendo-published Nintendo Switch 2 game in its first four days."

Nintendo specified that 810,000 of these copies were sold in Japan. This means the Ocarina of Time remake sold around 272,959 digital copies in the region, roughly a third of the game's total sales in the region.

With Sony's decision to end physical disc production in 2028, and Rockstar releasing a ton of copies of GTA 6 today that feature nothing more than a code in a box that will activate in a week's time, there's been a lot of debate around physical game releases as of late. However, it's clear there's still a market out there for them, even if it is shrinking.

Granted, Zelda's fortunes may have been helped by the fancy physical boxed copy that the game launched with, but even so, two-thirds of the game's sales in the region are certainly significant.

Xbox boss seemingly champions gen-AI development as she celebrates 25 years of Xbox: "A small team will be able to build worlds that today demand hundreds of developers"

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xbox leader Asha Sharma has alluded to harnessing the power of generative AI when making games, in a memo published this afternoon.

The memo, titled 25 years of Xbox, charts the growth of Microsoft from small beginnings through to the thousands-strong company it is today. But it's Sharma's comments at the end of the memo that are of particular interest.

While talking about what the next 25 years could bring, Sharma lamented that "too many ideas never become games" and that "too many games fall short of what their creators imagined". "For decades, this has been the unavoidable tradeoff of ambition," she wrote. "We must change this."

Sharma continued: "Throughout human history, the scale of what we could create was determined by the people, capital, and resources we could command. That relationship is beginning to break. A single person will soon be able to wield capabilities that once required entire nations. A small team will be able to build worlds that today demand hundreds of developers and hundreds of millions of dollars. And our largest studios will be able to pursue ambitions as far beyond today's games as Microsoft Flight Simulator 2024 is from its first entry in 1982."

There is no explicit mention of generative AI here, but the language is suggestive - "a single person will soon be able to wield capabilities that once required entire nations". Furthermore, Asha's memo follows last month's announcement of an Xbox Labs initiative that also sounded like it was based around generative AI, despite avoiding the phrase specifically.

This isn't a surprise, of course. Microsoft as a wider company is all-in on generative AI, with Copilot, and Sharma's background at Microsoft is in AI. But it's an area she's avoided speaking too enthusiastically about since her hiring as Xbox boss earlier this year. At the time, Sharma said she has "no tolerance for bad AI", and there's been such a lot else going on in the months since - including wide-ranging restructuring resulting in the loss of thousands of Xbox jobs - that the topic of generative AI has fallen into the background. Perhaps now we're seeing the sprouting seeds of it.

Orakl Oncology raises $34M to predict cancer drug results with AI

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Paris startup tests drugs on lab-grown copies of patients' tumours, then uses AI to forecast trial results.

Flapping Airplanes wants to train AI with less data and get more out of each chip.

Paris-based Orakl Oncology has raised a $34M Series A co-led by Norrsken VC and Singular. It grows copies of patients’ tumours in the lab, tests drugs on them and uses AI to predict trial results. It will expand into lung and gastric cancer.

Paris-based Orakl Oncology has raised a $34M Series A to predict how cancer drugs will perform before they reach patients, European Biotechnology reported. The round was co-led by Swedish investor Norrsken VC and French firm Singular, taking total funding to $50M.

Orakl was spun out of French cancer centre Gustave Roussy in 2023. It grows small copies of patients’ tumours in the lab, called organoids, and tests drugs on them. It then combines those results with patients’ medical records to train AI that predicts how a drug will do in clinical trials.

Norrsken says the system is 90% accurate. An April preprint, not yet peer-reviewed, tested the model on 23 past colorectal and pancreatic cancer trials. Its forecasts of how long patients went without their cancer worsening were off by 0.82 months on average.

It also picked the better treatment in seven of eight cases. But those tests looked back at trials that had already finished, and most involved standard chemotherapy. How the model handles newer drugs still needs to be proven.

The money will go into producing five times more lab data, expanding into lung and gastric cancers, and opening teams in the US and UK. Norrsken says 18 of the world’s 20 biggest drugmakers are in talks with Orakl, and in July it won rights to test four of Sanofi’s cancer drugs.

Orakl joins a wave of Paris startups using AI in biology, including Rivercell, which raised $25M in October. Singular also led a $20M round in Paris health startup Lucis this year, while US drug-design startup Chai Discovery raised $400M in July.

Professor Layton And The New World Of Steam English Voice Cast Revealed

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It has been a tense wait to see whether Level-5 would bring in real, human voice actors for the regional dubs of Professor Layton and The New World of Steam, or whether it would slip into the ol' AI that its CEO seems to bring up at every given opportunity. Fortunately, it's positive news.

The dev has today revealed the game's English voice cast, and it's headed up by Christopher Robin Miller and Lani Minella — the returning North American Layton and Luke voice actors from the main series. You can even hear a snippet of them in action on the game's website, and yes, the nostalgia is a welcome listen.

The returning actors will be joined by the likes of Amber Lee Connors (Persona 4 Revival, Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave, Unicorn Overlord), Nic Olsen (Genshin Impact), Arthur Romeo (Street Fighter 6, AI: The Somnium Files - nirvanA Initiative) and more.

Interestingly, Cat Protano (Xenoblade Chronicles X: Definitive Edition, Trails in the Sky) seems to be taking on the role of Luke in the UK dub, and not the previous main series actor, Maria Darling.

The website also showcases the cast list for the game's French, Italian, German and Spanish dubs, all of which use real voice actors — what a concept!

Pre-orders for the New World of Steam's PC and PS5 releases have gone live today, with Switch 1 and 2 pre-orders expected at a later date.

It's worth pointing out that the newly published Steam page doesn't feature the site's AI disclosure, which is required for all games that use visual or other in-game AI assets. The lack of a disclosure doesn't mean AI hasn't been used under the hood (CEO Akihiro Hino said it hasn't been used anywhere in the game), but the last few years following Level-5 have made us cautious at every turn.

Years after its initial reveal, Professor Layton and the New World of Steam finally got a firm release date back in the September Direct: 10th December. We're cautious about getting our expectations too high around it, but the returning voice cast goes a long way toward alleviating some initial fears.

Nothing’s screen-less new gadget really looks like yet another AI recorder

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nothing has been teasing its first dedicated AI product and, as we expected, it really looks like an AI recorder.

Last week, Nothing CEO Carl Pei announced that the company would be launching its first “AI-native” product that doesn’t have a screen and “couldn’t have been an app.” Now, Pei is back with a brief teaser for the product, captioned “Context doesn’t live in a chat box.”

Nothing is keeping the details of what this product is a secret for now, but it’s becoming painfully obvious that this is an AI recorder.

The green and red lights paired with what looks like a record button paint a very clear picture. As we pointed out initially, Pei’s first post heavily implied this was some type of audio device. He said:

Meanwhile, the context that matters most never reaches the model at all. Think about the last time someone said something worth keeping: an idea over coffee, a throwaway comment that unblocks your whole week. What did you do? “Hold on, let me write that down.” Out comes the phone, and by the time you look up, the moment has moved on without you. Or you don’t bother, and it’s gone by the end of the day.

There are many AI recorders already on the market, admittedly with varying levels of quality, so we’ll have to wait and see how Nothing’s differentiates itself.

Frida Kahlo’s Image Has Become a Global Brand. A Viral AI-Generated Ad Makes Things Worse.

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In our Frida Kahlo–obsessed moment, it’s may be difficult for some to remember that the Mexican artist almost died before she could change art history. In 1925, when she was just 18 years old, she was riding a bus that collided with a trolley, an accident that severely injured her pelvis, abdomen, and uterus. During the painful recovery, she took up painting. “I am not sick,” she said. “I am broken. But, I am happy to be alive as long as I can paint.”

A little over a century after the accident, the fashion brand Les Énervés, which has its roots in Paris and Bali, released an ad campaign that seemed determined to posthumously break Kahlo all over again, this time using AI. Related Articles Fundación Kahlo Launches $50,000 Prize for Emerging Mexican Artists Mexican Government Faces Legal Action over Its Handling of Art Collection Rich in Kahlos

In the bizarre ad, which was first posted to Instagram in October and started going viral on X this month, a bubblegum-chewing Kahlo recklessly drives a bus through Paris. Her passengers include the Mona Lisa and Vincent van Gogh, his severed ear replaced with a steel prosthetic. It’s worth noting that the Kahlo depicted here downs multiple drinks before getting behind the wheel; the real artist struggled with alcohol and drug addiction.

“It’s Frida, bitch,” she says, just before the soundtrack switches from an Édith Piaf song to Britney Spears’s “Gimme More.”

According to a release by Les Énervés, the ad was directed by the brand’s founder, Wakim Krari, and was produced using what is described as “AI, and a lot of patience.” The brand’s website adds that AI has been used to recreate Kahlo’s bus, this time as a Renault TN, which might have been seen in Paris when Kahlo staged an exhibition there in 1939.

Les Énervés has attempted to frame the ad as some sort of statement of reclamation. “A hundred years later, she is the one driving,” Les Énervés notes. “Vincent and Mona Lisa are in the back. Nothing is going to hit her this time.”

Not everyone has the same interpretation. One X post with 46,000 likes says the ad is “disrespecting the dead.” I concur.

Worse, it’s far from the first generated AI-generated Kahlo project to disrespect her memory. Another AI-generated video, also making the rounds on TikTok and X, finds Kahlo recast as a Spice Girl, singing “Wannabe” alongside the Mona Lisa, Vermeer’s model from Girl with a Pearl Earring, and the woman from Jan van Eyck’s Arnolfini Wedding. There are also quite a few Kahlo chatbots online, including one that implores viewers with this prompt: “Ask me about my struggles with physical pain, my relationship with Diego Rivera, or my feminist beliefs.”

It’s one thing to feed the Mona Lisa into AI, and quite another to use AI to mess with Kahlo, who carefully fashioned her own self-image in ways that resisted the social norms expected to guide a 20th-century Mexican woman. In her painted self-portraits, she consciously sported a darkened unibrow and facial hair men’s clothing, flouting gender expectations. She was famous, too, for wearing Tehuana skirts, positioning Indigenous Zapotec culture as central to Mexican identity. (Her unibrow also figures in the Les Énervés ad, here because Kahlo draws it onto the Mona Lisa, who has little to no eyebrows. “The woman who has too much of it gives some to the woman who has none,” the brand informs viewers.)

The nuances of her fashion are increasingly sandblasted by brands ranging from Barbie dolls to tequila bottles. Mari Carmen Ramírez, the curator of a show called “Frida: The Making of an Icon” that is now on view at Tate Modern in London, has said that Kahlo’s image has become “subsumed” by artists and activists, seemingly both for the better and the worse.

To me, all this AI-generated Kahlo content is subsuming the artist by another means. The reason to be concerned about all this, though, is only partly to do with the commercialization of Kahlo—it’s more about AI and the ways it can be used to distort, and perhaps even disregard, art history.

It might seem cute, for example, to place Kahlo at the center stage of art history, in such a way that sidelines her husband, the artist Diego Rivera, who features in the Énervés ad as trying to keep up with Kahlo while she walks down a rain-slicked street.But their 25-year relationship was more complicated than that: Kahlo was both deeply in love with and frequently frustrated by Rivera, who betrayed her by having an affair with her younger sister. The gesture here is a simple-minded one, easily made given that Kahlo is alive to comment. (She died at 47 in 1954, when she was nowhere near as famous as she is today.)

One is tempted to invoke Britney Spears, just as Les Énervés has done, and say to this brand: Leave Frida alone. Perhaps Les Énervés will do just that, but the brand has seemingly already found its next target. “The next chapter is coming soon, with a new muse and new things hidden in the frame,” the brand promises on its website. It reads like a threat.

Dark matter breakthrough coming? Euclid space telescope unveils its 1st look into the dark universe (video)

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Start exploring exclusive deals, expert advice and more

Space news, cosmic updates and expert insights straight to your inbox.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

On Thursday (Nov. 12), the Euclid space telescope released its first major batch of data, capturing the light of more than a billion objects over an area of sky as big as 9,500 full moons. The data release lays the foundation for one of the largest-ever 3D maps of the cosmos and many groundbreaking discoveries about the nature of dark energy and dark matter.

The European Space Agency's (ESA) Euclid space telescope has been called the "Dark Universe Detective" because of its capability to investigate the dual mysteries of dark matter, the universe's most mysterious stuff, and dark energy, the force accelerating the expansion of the universe. Collectively, these cosmic elements are sometimes referred to as the dark universe.

The new data release, designated DR1-Foundation, was built from 13 months of observations and is accompanied by a multitude of images, including large sky mosaics. It marks the first major release of mission data since Euclid launched in mid-2023.

"These are far more than beautiful pictures; today marks a scientific milestone in our study of the cosmos," ESA's Director of Science, Carole Mundell said in a statement. "This major Euclid data release represents the largest space-based survey of its kind, laying the foundation of future discovery in a new regime for astronomy and demonstrating the excellence of European technology, science and international collaboration."

The ESA put into raw numbers the sheer number of celestial objects viewed by the Dark Universe Detective and contained in DR1-Foundation. That includes light spectra from 160 million sources, over 900 million galaxies, and 60 million candidates for quasars, regions of space dominated by feeding supermassive black holes.

"This is a change in scale from Euclid's previous releases, which included a few days of observations over around 60 degrees squared of sky," ESA's Euclid project scientist Valeria Pettorino said. "We're now releasing more than a year of data covering a significantly larger area. This unlocks groundbreaking science."

Euclid can see such a vast number of cosmic objects thanks to the fact that its space-based position helps it avoid interference caused by Earth's atmosphere.

This includes clusters of galaxies and even larger superclusters. The study of these objects, some of the largest structures in the cosmos, can reveal how matter came together across the larger network of gas, dust and dark matter called the cosmic web.

"Euclid doesn't just look far back in time  — it does so across huge areas of sky," Euclid Consortium member Barbara Sartoris of JL Lagrange Laboratory, Nice, France, explained. "This gives us an unprecedented view of large samples of ancient galaxy clusters that were previously out of reach. "We will explore these in the coming months to learn how these giants grew, and decipher what they reveal about the dark universe."

Another exciting element of the new Euclid data release is the 15,000 strong gravitational lensing candidates catalogued by the space telescope. Gravitational lensing is an element of the universe first explained in Einstein magnum opus, general relativity, in 1915.

General relativity says that objects with mass cause the very fabric of space to warp, and that gravity arises from this warping. But there is another consequence. When light passes this curvature, its usually straight path is curved. That means when light from a single background source passes an object with great mass, like a galaxy or galaxy cluster, a strong gravitational lens, it can arrive at our telescopes at different times.

As a result of this strong lensing, background sources can be greatly amplified. It is an effect that the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) has been using to great effect to study galaxies in the early universe.

Thus, the prospect of mapping 15,000 strong gravitational lenses is monumental, especially in studying the rate at which the universe is expanding and thus the effect of dark energy.

"Strong lensing provides unique insight into the dark universe, allowing us to 'weigh' individual galaxies, including their invisible dark matter, and explore how the universe is expanding," Natalie Lines of the University of Portsmouth, U.K., and member of the Euclid Consortium, said. "Over 10 years ago, we forecast that Euclid would revolutionize the field of strong lens discovery. That day is finally here."

Arguably, the most exciting thing about DR1-Foundation is that Euclid is just getting started. The area of sky covered from spring 2024 to spring 2025 represents just 14% of Euclid's target for its entire six-year survey.

That means whatever scientists discover from this data release could be the tip of the iceberg in terms of how the Dark Universe Detective will change our understanding of the cosmos.

"DR1-Foundation includes raw data, calibrated images, catalogues, and spectra over a huge sky area, with enough statistics touncover even rare objects," Valeria said. "It will be used to map the large-scale structure of the universe in unprecedented detail.

Robert Lea is a science journalist in the U.K. whose articles have been published in Physics World, New Scientist, Astronomy Magazine, All About Space, Newsweek and ZME Science. He also writes about science communication for Elsevier and the European Journal of Physics. Rob holds a bachelor of science degree in physics and astronomy from the U.K.’s Open University. Follow him on Twitter @sciencef1rst.

Grow Your Own Christmas Decorations – 7 Plants to Add to Your Garden for Festive Displays Year After Year

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

Expert tips for growing fruit, vegetables and herbs at home.

Sign up to get fresh gardening ideas, inspiration and expert advice delivered straight to your inbox.

What Your Hydrangeas Need in November – 5 Ways to Get Plants Winter-Ready and Kickstart Your Best Blooms Ever

7 Foolproof Plants Anyone Can Propagate From Cuttings in November – Grow More of Your Favorite Shrubs for Free

8 Easy Landscape Designer Tricks to Transition Your Garden From Fall into Winter

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The holidays are rushing in, but if you grow your own festive decorations you can create unique centerpieces and homegrown garlands that are the envy of all your neighbors

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The holiday season is fast approaching, bringing the annual scramble for festive boughs. wreaths, garlands, and mantelpiece greenery. But why buy store-bought greenery that will just end up in yard waste bags by January? Plastic greenery may be used again, but it doesn’t bring the same charm. Besides, there’s a far more rewarding alternative: growing your own living holiday decor outside your back door.

Growing plants that can help you see in the holiday season in style can deliver dual rewards. Through the year, these shrubs, trees, and aromatic herbs provide texture, wildlife shelter, and vibrant winter color. Come December, they supply an abundant, sustainable harvest of natural Christmas decorations in the form of lush foliage, pretty berries, and uplifting fragrance for seasonal wreaths, table runners, and gift wrapping.

Most of these perennial performers are resilient, adaptable, and low-maintenance once established. Bring authentic seasonal beauty indoors with these gorgeous double-duty beauties. These winter garden plants add effortless elegance, joy and character to your festive displays, and generally they need very little upkeep, freeing you up to enjoy the season. Here are 7 standout plants to add to your yard for easy homegrown holiday displays you can harvest for years to come.

Your USDA hardiness zone has some bearing on the plants you add for clippings that can festoon your festive garland and centerpieces. In mild and transitional zones, any hardy evergreens and deciduous shrubs planted now will establish strong roots over winter while top growth rests. In colder northern regions where frost arrives early, tender herbs thrive indoors in sunny containers, ready to move outside next spring.

Most evergreen conifers, hollies, and woodland shrubs thrive in slightly acidic, humus-rich soil with reliable drainage. Meanwhile, Mediterranean herbs demand sharp grit and full sun. When planting woody shrubs in November, dig a hole twice as wide as the root ball. Backfill with native soil amended with organic compost, and water deeply to settle any air pockets before the ground freezes.

Apply a 2-inch (5cm) layer of shredded bark mulch around the base, keeping it clear of the main stem, to insulate roots against freeze-thaw cycles. I like Back to the Roots Organic Premium Wood Mulch, available from Amazon, to help lock in soil moisture and regulate soil temperature. In zones 6 and colder, verify that soil remains workable and unfrozen before planting. If hard freezes have already locked the earth, hold hardy specimens in a cold frame or pot until spring.

When harvesting for holiday arrangements, never strip entire limbs or harvest heavily from young shrubs during the first two years of establishment. Instead, take selective snips evenly from around the plant. Always cut just above an outward-facing bud or lateral branch, using sharp, sanitized bypass pruners to prevent winter disease.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

To keep cut greenery lush indoors, wait until you are ready to craft before harvesting, or submerge freshly cut stem ends in a bucket of cool water in an unheated garage for 24-48 hours to hydrate fully. Keep finished wreaths and garlands away from roaring fireplaces, radiators, and heating vents to prevent premature needle drop and fire hazards. Mist your arrangements lightly with water every few days. Hopefully the following plants will give you plenty of season’s greetings…

Nothing captures traditional Christmas decor like the glossy foliage and scarlet berries of American holly (Ilex opaca). Hardy across zones 5-9, this stately native evergreen provides year-round privacy and winter shelter for songbirds, before lending its iconic branches to holiday mantels and door wreaths. Plant in full sun to partial shade in moist, slightly acidic soil. It can reach 15-30 feet (4.5-9m) tall, so allow ample elbow room or choose compact cultivars like Maryland Dwarf.

You must plant at least one pollinating male cultivar (such as Jersey Knight) within 40 feet of berry-producing female trees (Satyr Hill or Miss Helen) to guarantee fruit set. Harvest small, 8-12-inch (20-30cm) sprigs evenly around the tree using sharp pruners. Dip stems in water overnight before wiring into garlands. Note that holly berries are toxic to pets and humans if ingested, so display out of reach of dogs and toddlers. You can buy American Holly from Nature Hills at a discount if you hurry.

For winter drama, few shrubs rival winterberry holly. Unlike its evergreen cousins, this rugged native deciduous holly drops its leaves in fall to reveal bare branches densely studded with luminous, bright red berries that persist well into midwinter. Thriving across zones 3-9, winterberry (Ilex verticillata) is ideal for damp, low-lying, or poorly drained spots where most other woody ornamentals rot. It prefers full sun for maximum fruit production, as well as acidic, organic-rich soil.

Winterberry also requires male and female plants for pollination (and hence berries). Pair prolific female cultivars like Winter Red or compact Berry Poppins with a male pollinator such as Southern Gentleman or Mr. Poppins. Cut stems look magnificent in tall floor vases, outdoor porch planters, and minimalist wreaths paired with silver-blue cedar. Harvest selectively once leaves have fallen, leaving plenty of fruit for robins and cedar waxwings. If you fancy a golden variation on the classic winterberry, you can buy Yellow Winterberry Holly from Amazon.

When winter strips color from the yard, red twig dogwood (Cornus sericea) brings blazing crimson bark that looks electrifying against fresh snow. Hardy in zones 2-7, this vigorous native tolerates everything from boggy clay to average loam in full sun or light shade. Look for vibrant Cardinal or compact Arctic Fire, which stays under 4 feet (1.2m) tall and fits neatly into small suburban borders. You can currently buy Bailey Red Twigged Dogwood from Nature Hills at a staggering 40% discount.

The most dramatic bark develops on 1-2-year-old shoots, as older wood gradually turns brown. Cut out up to one third of the oldest, thickest stems down to the ground in late fall. This renewal pruning stimulates a flush of vivid new canes next spring, while providing structural crimson wands for your holiday decor. Weave flexible young stems into evergreen wreaths for classic contrast, or arrange upright branches in weighted urns alongside white pine and pinecones.

Junipers deliver unmistakable fragrance alongside feathery, silver-blue foliage studded with dusty, berry-like cones. Thriving in zones 3-9 depending on species, junipers range from low-spreading groundcovers (like Blue Rug) to graceful upright evergreens like Eastern red cedar (Juniperus virginiana) and blue-tinged Rocky Mountain juniper (Wichita Blue). Plant in full sun and sharp, well-drained soil. Once established, junipers are drought-tolerant and shrug off winter winds. You can get Wichita Blue Juniper from Trees for a compact holiday-friendly choice.

Their supple boughs are tailor-made for weaving lush garlands over doorways, shaping rustic table runners, and layering texture into mixed evergreen wreaths. When harvesting branches, prune lightly from the outer tips and never cut back into old, bare interior wood. Dunk cut sprigs in cool water for 24 hours before crafting, and wear sleeves to guard against potential skin irritation from the resinous needles.

Bridging the gap between holiday decor and festive feasting, here comes rosemary. Its pine-scented foliage brings instant charm to winter tablescapes and mini wreaths, especially paired with dried orange slices and cinnamon sticks. Rosemary (Salvia rosmarinus) is reliably perennial outdoors in zones 8-10, where it thrives in lean alkaline soil. If you are in zone 7 or colder, grow upright cultivars like Tuscan Blue or Arp in terracotta containers. You can buy Arp Rosemary Indoor Christmas Plants from Plant Addicts. Just remember to transition plants outside when weather permits.

Move pots indoors to a bright, south-facing windowsill before hard fall freezes arrive, watering sparingly only when the top two inches (5cm) of soil feel dry. Harvesting sprigs in December encourages bushy branching, but never remove more than one-third of the plant at once. Any leftover trimmings from wreath-making sessions can be used in holiday roast potatoes, focaccia, and mulled cider. Win-win!

For a modern, frosted aesthetic that elevates traditional red-and-green palettes, try silver dollar eucalyptus (Eucalyptus cinerea). Prized by floral designers for its dusty leaves and camphor aroma, this fast-growing Australian native is hardy outside in zones 8-11. In cooler climates, grow it as a chic container specimen that can come into a cool, bright sunroom or frost-free conservatory before temperatures dip below 25°F (-4°C). You can buy Silver Dollar Eucalyptus Trees from Trees with a 20% discount if you quote the code at checkout.

Eucalyptus demands full sun and gritty, fast-draining soil. In late fall, selectively prune back overly long, whippy shoots by up to a third. This shapes the plant before winter rest, rewarding you with supple stems for cascading garlands, mantel swags, and vase displays. Unlike conifers that eventually shed, eucalyptus dries gracefully indoors. Just let the cut stems dry before using them in arrangements, hanging them upside down in a cool, dark room for 2 weeks.

Eastern white pine (Pinus strobus) is quintessential holiday greenery. Instantly recognizable by its soft, feathery bundles of blue-green needles, this stately conifer thrives in zones 3-8. Plant in full sun or light shade in slightly acidic, well-drained soil. Give it generous space, too. Standard trees can soar 50-80 feet (15-24m) tall, but compact cultivars like Nana or Blue Shag suit smaller yards. You can get 30% off an American Beauties’ Eastern White Pine Trees from Nature Hills.

Growing white pine for holiday decor rewards patience. Allow newly planted trees 3-5 years to establish before harvesting, and never cut the central leader shoot, as it can ruin the tree's natural symmetry. When mature, snip 12-18-inch (30-45cm) lateral side tips around the lower and middle branches. You can weave its flexible stems into lush banister garlands and door wreaths, while its perfume fills the home.

Growing a holiday garden is a great way to get the most out of your plants, but it does require time and patience. Choose the right plants for your garden and help them get established strongly. Don’t rush the process and harvest too much too soon, and you’ll be rewarded with years of lush, natural décor.

Need more ideas for getting the most from your plants, indoors and out, and looking for the best seasonal expert advice delivered straight to your inbox? Sign up for the free Gardening Know How Newsletter!

Mary Ellen Ellis has been gardening for over 20 years. With degrees in Chemistry and Biology, Mary Ellen's specialties are flowers, native plants, and herbs.

The dust hasn't even settled yet after Jensen Huang's resignation as Nvidia CEO, and the US government is already making it pretty clear where it stands on who gets the job next.

Nvidia CFO Colette Kress has taken up the role of CEO following Huang's departure, but only on an interim basis. The Nvidia board will have to decide on a full-time replacement to take over from Huang, who had led the company since its founding in 1993.

The chairman of the Select Committee on the Chinese Communist Party, John Moolenaar, has put out a statement on the high-profile departure. It reads:

“Jensen Huang’s firing from the company he founded is an unfortunate end that could have been avoided."

"It is in our national interest for Nvidia and our other AI champions to succeed, but American companies should never jeopardize our nation’s security by giving Chinese companies access to their most advanced chips. Nvidia must now change its corporate practices and appoint leadership that makes national security its top priority."

Moolenaar also says that Congress should aim to increase control over exports of top AI chips to China, with further export control legislation in the national defense bill by the end of the year.

Some in the US government are more opposed to China than others, and the China committee most among them.
